## Rothé Valley Village

Rothé Valley Village was the most prominent of the villages and farms in Rothé Valley as of the late 15 century DR.

Geography
The village itself was positioned on the High Road, not far from the nearby Never River.

Government
Befitting a small settlement of the North, the village was led by a single mayor elected from among the local community.

History
The village was founded in the late 13 century, in the . Following the cataclysmic events of the Spellplague a century later, when the High Road around Neverwinter was cut off, the village was largely forgotten about by the surrounding world.

Strangely enough, a mysterious tunnel appeared near town in the . Baffled by its sudden appearance, local townsfolk went to investigate, only to learn it was formed by the traveling wizard Rucksam on a drunken bet with a old farmer.

As the city of Neverwinter entered a period of reconstruction during the latter half of the 15 century, Rothé Valley and its village came to provided much-needed food to the increasing number of people that called the city home.

As the greater Rothé Valley faced raids from drow slavers in the, the village itself was overrun by spiders that were controlled by the dark elves. Some folk boarded themselves up in their homes, while others were not so fortunate and were captured or worse. While some drow used magical means to hold hostage family members of Mayor Gunderson under the guard of the Daughter of Lolth, others formed a web of dark energy over the town while deathweavers prepared some sort of horrific, dark ritual involving the captured townsfolk that would aide Lolth's efforts to create a new form of the Weave under her control.

Fortunately for Rothé Valley Village, Neverwinter's Rangers along with Mayor Gunderson worked together to elicit the aid of an adventurer from Neverwinter to defeat the Drow expedition, and take the fight to their home in the Underdark.

Rumors & Legends
Throughout the Present Age, rumors in town circulated about "shadow-people" emerging at night to kidnap villages and abduct them away from town.

## Roar of the king

Roar of the king was a divine evocation spell used by specialty priests devoted to Nobanion called Firemanes, enabling them to shout with ear-piercing power.

Effects
This spell boosted the vocal strength of the caster, allowing them to shriek with ear-piercing power like a male lion, temporarily deafening any creature within  in front of the caster. The roar was so powerful that it would also physically harm targets and shatter brittle equipment on their person.

Roar of the king was not capable of penetrating a silence, 15' radius spell. The roar could be heard up to a mile away, making it an effective way raise an alarm. The spell could be cast repeatedly during the same day.

Components
In addition to verbal components, this spell required as material components a drop of honey, a piece of cinnamon bark, a small piece of meat, and a piece of amber worth at least 20 gp to cast.

## Roar of Shadows

The Roar of Shadows was a titanic conflict between the avatars of Nobanion, the King of Lions, and Malar, the Beastlord, during the Time of Troubles.

Location
This battle occurred within the Gulthmere Forest of the Vilhon Reach.

History
When gods were forced to walk on Toril, in the, the avatar of Malar invaded the Gulthmere Forest—the territory guarded by the Lion God, the avatar of Nobanion. Malar's plan of slaughtering druids of the forest was foiled by the lion deity, and the two clashed in a rancorous battle named the Roar of Shadows. The battle was joined by ranking members of the Emerald Enclave, aiding Nobanion, and in the end, Malar was pushed out of the forest and driven north.

Nobanion secured the forest as the seat of his power on Toril, eventually opening a permanent portal to the Beastlands.

## Rod of splendor

A rod of splendor was an enchanted rod capable of turning its wielder into a natural leader.

Powers
Anyone who carried one of these rods was granted the charisma of a natural leader so long as the rod remained in their possession. The rod also created an illusion making any garments or equipment worn by the wielder to appear to be of the highest quality. The wielder could activate the rod to actually transform their current clothing into a fine outfit complete with gems and jewels. The ensemble would be valued at 7,000–10,000 gp. This new apparel would remain in existence so long as the wielder did not have it forcibly removed from their person or try to sell it. The clothing would simply vanish in either case. However, the wielder could give the clothing as a gift to another person.

A rod of splendor could also create a luxurious pavilion of silk complete with extravagant furnishings and delicious food. The size of the pavilion was between –, allowing the entertainment for up to 100 people. The tent would last for one full day before vanishing but could be maintained by expending another of the rod's charges. Any items taken from inside of the tent would disappear when the tent vanished.

These items could not be recharged.

Notable Owners
Imam Lelia, a dwarven pragmatist from Huzuz, carried a rod of splendor.

## Roland the Minstrel

Roland the Minstrel was a minstrel in the Oasis of the White Palm the time of Khalitharius' release in .

Possessions
Roland's only possession of note was a lute.

Activities
Roland sometimes wandered the oasis, but from dawn to dusk he was just plucking notes out of his lute in the common room of the Sandvoyagers' Guild. Though he was secretive and didn't answer many questions, he preferred to give the impression he knew a great deal.

What he knew was limited to a few facts. First amongst them was that Martek had originally created the ruins under the Oasis as well as those at Phoenix, long before they were dedicated to Set; he still chose to hide his Star Gems in them. He also knew that Martek, the greatest mage of his time, had foreseen the future and left a gift to the Sultanate of the tribes, giving them the knowledge of where the Great Good slept, to be loosed when the Great Evil (almost certainly Khalitharius) came. He also knew that the ancient city of Phoenix was located west by northwest of the Oasis. He knew that Martek had delivered unto the Pharaohs of his day the five gems known as the Star Gems of Mo-Pelar, Shah-Pelar, Khan-Pelar, Aga-Pelar, and Melos-Pelar, items of great mystery, but his greatest creation was actually his own citadel.

Finally, he knew a bit of Martek's Epic.

## Robe of scintillating colors

A robe of scintillating colors was an enchanted garment whose colors constantly shifted.

Powers
In order for the magic of these robes to function the wearer had to possess sufficient intelligence and wisdom. The robe would shift colors and patterns with mesmerizing hues. This shed light within a  sphere around the robe and could even hypnotize any potential enemies so they would stare helplessly at the robe's display. Enemies able to resist the robe's hypnotic effect still found it more difficult to hit the wearer because of the dizzying display. Some versions of robes of scintillating colors also allowed the wearer to displace themselves once per day.

History
Robe of scintillating colors were believed to originate from the kingdom of Halruaa, created by a Lliira-worshiper Persephenon. Rare among fellow Halruaan wizards, Persephenon possessed the gift of sorcery. This talent, his eccentric personality, and veneration of the Joybringer goddess led to societal stigma among the kingdom's wizards. Despite being a pariah, Persephenon eventually worked his way to the position of a skyship captain. Thankful for the opportunity, Persephenon created the first robe of scintillating colors as a tribute to his deity to paint rainbows as he traversed the skies.

This display of flamboyancy eventually cost Persephenon respect and resulted in his exile from Halruaa. However, the scintillating sorcerer was welcomed by open-minded halfling of Luiren, where he found a new home and started creating copies of the colorful robes that once cost him so much.

Notable Owners
 Casimir, a wicked human wizard from Toril, wore a robe of scintillating colors.
 Korbus Brightjewel, a gnome jeweler who lived in Daggerford, owned a robe of scintillating colors.
 Timon Silvertongue, a powerful enchanter, who enjoyed exploring Faerûn.
 Vejoni H. Wyzz, a planeswalking wizard who perched his extraplanar tent just outside the town of Liam's Hold in 1372 DR.
 Viallis Yellowcrest, leader of the Burnt Tongue Cult.

A rod of alertness was a magic item that helped the wielder pierce illusions.

These rods resembled footman's maces with eight flanges on their head.

Powers
When used as a weapon, a rod of alertness made it slightly easier for the wielder to strike and harm their enemy, while also making them more alert to surprise attacks.

The rod enabled the wielder to detect alignment, evil, good, illusion, lies, magic, and invisibility when gripped tightly. If planted into the ground, the rod could sense any dangerous creatures within a  radius. Each flange on the rod's head would emit light to a range of  while protecting the wielder with a prayer in a  radius. Lastly, the rod could animate up to 16 objects selected by the wielder that were placed around the rod.

Rods of the ghost were enchanted rods that could turn their wielder ethereal for a short period of time.

## Rogues of Tilverton

The Rogues of Tilverton, who were also simply called the Rogues, was the only thieves' guild found in the city of Tilverton in the .

Activities
There were roughly 70 Rogues as of 1358 DR. They operated in the sewers beneath the city, as well as Grimwald's Revenge which provided secret access to the sewers. Some of the local merchants aided the Rogues in order to prevent thefts in their shops, and Gahlaerd Mossmere kept a safehouse for them and provide magical services.

They kept on a low-level conflict with the Fire Knives in the city, trying to drive out the far more murderous organization. They wished to keep the status quo in Tilverton and Cormyr at large, and had no desire to upend things by allowing King Azoun Obarskyr IV or other important personages to be killed.

Base of Operations
The Rogues' base was an expansive underground complex hidden under Tilverton, connected to the sewers and other locations by tunnels. Within, they had storerooms, barracks, training facilities, a shrine of Mask, and a treasury, as well as a luxurious audience hall, office, quarters and library for the Guildmaster.

History
The Rogues were led by Artur Grossman's father, who was slain by the Fire Knives after allowing them to take refuge in the city after they were purged from Cormyr in 1341 DR. Artur took control from that point through to 1367 DR, when he disappeared and was replaced by his daughter Lharae Grossman.

Members
In Artur's time, the guild had a great many members, including but not limited to:
 Guildmaster Artur "the Fat Man" Grossman
 Lharae "the Lithe" Grossman
 Fluffy and Bojangles, Artur's beloved dire wolf pets.
 Cutthroat McGill, a skilled thief.
 Thalia, a skilled thief.
 Four trusted senior thieves who acted as sergeants for the rest.
 45 thieves of some skill.
 Four heavily-equipped guards.
 Six ogres who acted as door-guards and bruisers.
 20 servants who worked in the guild's hideout and waited on Artur.

## Roline Tark

Roline "the Hand" Tark was a caravan guard from the city of Daggerford who made a fortune selling lizardfolk teeth to collectors, sometime before 1372 DR.

History
Roline wielded her enchanted mace against the lizardfolk, who often attacked merchant caravans of Daggerford. The weapon was crafted specifically to battle against the increased threat of the scaled humanoids of the Lizard Marsh. With time, Roline "the Hand" Tark started collecting teeth from the lizardfolk she defeated while protecting her employers' caravans. Later in life, she made a significant fortune selling the teeth to collectors. The name of the mace—the Teeth Taker—was given to the weapon by the very lizardfolk it was used against. It was a gesture of respect of the magic mace and of Roline Tark.

## Roju

​
The roju was the Council of Elders of Wa, one of the three primary offices of the shogunate.

Organization
Members of the roju were selected from among the fudai daimyo, the so-called "house daimyo" who were most loyal to the shogun, and approved by him. This was a very honored role in Wa society, and to hold the position, one must have owned property equivalent to at leaat 25,000 koku of rice.

The leader of the roju held the title of kahan.

Activities
The primary role of the roju was to supervise the lesser daimyo. They also performed other administrative tasks, such as the regulation of official currency, overseeing internal government affairs within Uwaji Province, administration of government shrines and temples, and the commissioning of official maps.

The kahan had the additional role of stamping the shogun's official seal upon all government documents.

## Robe from another world

The so-called robe from another world was a strange and alien enchanted robe found by a planeswalking wizard Vejoni H. Wyzz.

Powers
The robe from another world was imbued with several sorcerous enchantments. It improved the wearer's defenses and reactions when dealing with natural and supernatural dangers, as well as improved their audible perception. Additionally, the robe from another world allowed the wearer to store a single spell within its magical fibers. The stored spell could be replaced at will and discharged twice per day.

History
Sometime before 1372 DR, Vejoni H. Wyzz, a planar-traveling wizard, discovered this magic robe during one of his travels. No other information on the origins of the robe from another world, its plane, or its creator remain unrevealed. In 1372 DR, Vejoni placed his tent on the Trade Way just outside Liam's Hold. The tent itself led to the wizard's pocket plane of existence from where, Vejoni H. Wyzz operated his store while he stayed on Toril. The robe from another world was available for purchase from the planeswalker.

## Robe of energy

Robes of energy were enchanted magical garments infused with a simple magic missile spell usable by wizards and sorcerers only.

Powers
Robes of energy were permeated and infused with the sorcery of magic missiles. This enchanted the robes to be able to discharge a powerful version of the magic missile spell once per day and gave the wearer immunity to the same spell. They also significantly enhanced their concentration required for spellcasting. Finally, the robes glowed with a red light, illuminating  around it.

History
Robes of energy were originally invented by a female member of the Arcane Brotherhood of Luskan. She experimented with magical infusing and discovered an unexpectedly powerful enchantment in the process. The garments of her crafting became popular in the black markets and streets of Luskan sometime before 1372 DR. Eventually, the Arcane Brotherhood discovered that she was selling the secrets of the Host Tower of the Arcane and their wrath was horrible. The creator of the robes of energy was roasted slowly over an open flame.

By 1372 DR, copies of these robes were present in several cities along the Sword Coast.

## Rod of frost

Rods of frost were enchanted rods that allowed the wielder to fire rays of freezing cold.

Description
Rods of frost were  transparent glassteel rods bound with iron bands and weighing .

Powers
When activated, these rods emitted a  ray of frost. The magical cold of the ray extinguished all flames it touched and froze the entire affected area. The ray of frost could be blasted three times per day.

Creation
A skilled crafter of wondrous items could create such rods with the use ray of frost spell. The process usually took two days and cost 700 gp.

History
In the late 14 century DR, rods of frost could be found throughout the Sword Coast region, notably in the city of Neverwinter

Notable Users
Jaroo, a gnomish wizard and a teacher of the Adventurer's Academy of Neverwinter. He crafted many rods of frost to be given out to his most skilled students circa 1372 DR.
Vejoni H. Wyzz, a planeswalking wizard who sold many enchanted items, including rods of frost. He briefly visited the hamlet of Liam's Hold in 1372 DR.

The Rot Grubs were a criminal organization within Ordulin as of the .

The Rot Grubs viewed themselves as revolutionaries who performed all kinds of criminal acts in the name of the "true king" of Sembia, though they certainly seemed far more interested in illegally acquiring wealth for themselves.

These criminals were secretly backed by the Zhentarim in order to promote civil unrest in the area.

## Robes of the Dark Maiden

Robes of the Dark Maiden were traditional enchanted robes worn by the Eilistraean Order of the Dark Maiden.

Powers
Robes of the Dark Maiden bore a potent defensive +5 enchantment, granter the wearer immunity to mundane and magical fear, as well as granted or enchanted their darkvision. Due to the robes' enchantments tailored specifically for the drow, they could only be worn by creatures of elven blood.

History
These robes were ensorcelled to be worn by the monk devotees of the drow goddess Eilistraee. The monastic Order of the Dark Maiden worked to promote their deity's values - peace and harmony between the drow and the inhabitants of the surface world. Even though peaceful by nature, they were swift to smite evil aggressors and the robes' enchantments kept the monks safe.

By the late 14 century DR, several of these magic robes left the hands fo their drow owners and entered a wider circulation in the regions of the Sword Coast and the Moonsea.

## Rolan

Rolan was a wizard apprentice from Elturel that was arranged to study under the esteemed mage Lorroakan of Baldur's Gate during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Rolan had two black horns that gently curved back and up.

Personality
Not one without ambition, Rolan sought to be the "greatest wizard" in all of Faerûn.

Relationships
He had no family by blood relation but had a close bond with fellow tieflings Lia and Cal. While insecure about whether they truly felt the same, he considered them his siblings.

History
As of the, Rolan's apprenticeship with Lorroakan was set to begin. Unfortunately, his home city of Elturel was pulled down into Avernus in the Nine Hells. While this proved only a temporary impediment to his higher education in the arcane arts, the expulsion of all tieflings from Elturel was much more problematic. Rolan was waylaid for some time alongside the other tieflings traveling to Baldur's Gate under the leadership of Zevlor and forced to take shelter at the Emerald Grove, further delaying his inevitable rise as the preeminent spellcaster of the Realms.

Following a goblinoid attack on the Emerald Grove, Rolan got into an argument with Lia about whether or not they should help defend the druid's grove to flee for Baldur's Gate while they still had the opportunity.

## Rogarsheim

Rogarsheim, sometimes called Norland Town, was the capital city of the Northlander Kingdom of Norland on the island of Norland in the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
The city was located on the Bay of Norland along the coast of the Sea of Moonshae near the center of the island. It lay within a sheltered cove and lacked a deep-water harbor.

Defenses
Rogarsheim boasted the largest fleet among the dozen small kingdoms of Norland, consisting of six longships each crewed by forty experienced fighters. The fearsome warriors of Rogarsheim were sometimes known as the Stormbanner Wolves.

In the mid–14 century DR, their forces included a company of horsemen known as the Bloodriders, who were said to be the most elite mounted warriors among the Northlanders.

Trade
Most of Rogarsheim's industry was centered around fishing, hunting, and shipbuilding. The city was a major Northlander port for trade with far off Waterdeep and Ruathym. The lack of a traditional harbor meant that large ships had to drop anchor several hundred yards (meters) from the coast and transport goods ashore via boat. Meanwhile, Northlander longships simply sailed to the city's gravel beaches and were pulled up to park above the waterline.

Inhabitants
The Northlanders of Rogarsheim were famed for their warlike attitude, suspicion toward outsiders, and insular culture. Even so, they could be gracious hosts for esteemed visitors.

King Grunnarch the Red ruled Norland from his lodge in Rogarsheim in the mid–14 century DR.

Jarl Rault "the Wise" ruled Norland from Stormbanner Hold in Rogarsheim in the late 15 century DR. The population of Rogarsheim was a mix of Northlanders and Ffolk. There were plenty of dwarves here as well who were eager to mine the north and South Jotunhammer Mountains. It was also not uncommon to see fey creatures from Gwynneth who came to Rogarsheim to trade.

Ronassic was a human historian from the famous Outer Planes city of Sigil.

At some point Ronassic recorded a legend from the sahuagin of Toril about a mighty megalodon known as Iakhovas, which proclaimed him to be "The One Who Swims With Sekolah," the shark deity that was worshiped by them. ## Rokstang

Rokstang was a city in Tabot.

Geography
It stood in northern Tabot, near the headwaters of one of the rivers there. A road from Rokstang joined that linking Fij and Draya to the Lokar Pass and Ji far to the south.

Trade
Clay was gathered from the lands south of Rokstang. This was made into pottery by the lamas at Pokarr Gompa, and their works were renowned throughout Tabot.

History
Rokstang was inundated by a great flood in, before Mo Kin, Abbot of Pokaar, used one of the golden eggs of Ghastar to stop the flood.

Later, workers excavating a deep irrigation canal in Rokstang unearthed an extensive network of caverns. Furthermore, these contained a ruined city built entirely of brass that seemed to have suffered some great catastrophe. Afterward, the river began to dry up, possibly due to freezing high in the mountains, which led the lands around Rokstang to suffer drought. Believing a curse to have befallen Rokstang, in, the dzong-pon (sheriff) of the district sought courageous warriors to join him on an expedition into the brass city to end it.

Rumors & Legends
A blind beggar here told a tale that he and his companions had discovered a lost mine, stuffed with diamonds and other gemstones, deep in the Po Yul Dzayul Range. But a wrathful spirit attacked and slew them, and the beggar barely got away with his life. Blinded by either the spirit or the sun, he wandered a glacier for days before being found and taken to Rokstang, where he still lived in 2607.

## Rodolpho Wianar

Rodolpho Wianar was a nobleman and a cousin of Eles Wianar, the Lord of Arrabar.

History
In the Flamerule of the, Rodolpho attended his cousin's lush annual festival - the Night of Ghosts. At some point during the celebration, the man snuck away into the palace gardens of Generon to have a romantic rendezvous with a lady. At the same time, three young scions of Arrabar's noble houses, Adyan Mercatio, Horial Rohden, and Vambran Matrell, were playing around with Vambran's bejeweled crossbow, shooting at plantain fruits. One stay bolt missed the tree and embedded itself in Rodolpho's chest, killing him. Eventually, the nobleman's body was discovered by the man's paramour and friends, who were confronted by a masked assassin. The assassin shot at the guests and fled, pursued by the Municipal Guard of Arrabar. The assassin's identity was not discovered - Xaphira Matrell, beloved aunt of Vambran Matrell. Xaphira was a mercenary commander and decided to take the fall, saving her family and her nephew. She fled the city, leaving the Lord of Arrabar to start a headhunt for the murderer of his family member.

Eleven years later, Xaphira Matrell returned to Arrabar to aid her family against a plot that was sure to ruin the family. She shared some vital information regarding Rodolpho Wianar's death in the process. Vamran's stray bolt did not kill him. Someone stole several bolts from the young man's quiver and framed him. That became evident when Xaphira Matrell fled the guard. She was shot in the leg with the identical bolt that was retrieved from the dead nobleman. Rodolpho's death was part of a bigger plan orchestrated by those who sought to plunge Arrabar into war.

## Roasted beef

Roasted beef was a dish made from a sizable chunk of meat from a bovine beast common across Faerûn.

Description
Roasted beef was considered properly prepared when it was thoroughly cooked yet remained drippingly moist.

Availability
In Arrabar, Vilhon Reach, roasted beef was a popular dish served smothered in gravy. Beef roast was one of the best dishes on the menu available at aszraun called Dark She Looks Upon Me.
In Arabel, Cormyr, a plateful of sliced roast beef could be purchased at the Hungry Man restaurant.
Maid of the Moors in the town of Mornbryn's Shield in the Savage Frontier served a roast beef platter for 6 cp and a whole roast 3 gp.
In the city of Neverwinter, roasted beef prime rib was cooked in cognac, mustard, white wine, and whole peppercorns. The roast was then sliced and drowned in the sauce it was cooked in.
In the city of the Ravens Bluff, the tavern called The Seaside Salvage Company served a wide variety of simple roasts, including beef, valued at 4 silver coins per serving.
In Sembia, Selgauntan roasted beef was often served with wine, carrots, onions, pickled asparagus in a spicy Turmish cream sauce, accompanied by oxtail soup and fried wheat cake smothered in jellied pears.
In Waterdeep, the City of Splendors, the Master of the Kitchens of House Cragsmere, Klonstahansz Almurrhand, was renowned for his masterfully prepared moist and juicy roast beef and roast boar. The Cookhouse Hall of Waterdeep's Dock Ward served food to all who asked for a hot meal. Roast beef was among the simple dishes that were given out to the needy.

History
In the, the Lady Lord of Eveningstar, Tessaril Winter, welcomed the Knights of Myth Drannor with a feast of roast beef and Sembian semi-sweet wine.

In the 14 century DR, guests of the monastery of The Great Mother's House at Berbusk were often treated with a feast that included chicken, roast beef, pork, sausages, and various fruits and vegetables, all raised by the monks themselves.

## Roaringshore

Roaringshore was a small, isolated village on the Sword Coast in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Geography
The village nestled in a small deepwater cove on the coast, with the Winding Water river and Lathtarl's Lantern located north of the village and Baldur's Gate to the south.

Government
Roaringshore was known for being a lawless place, much like the neighboring village of Lathtarl's Lantern to the north.

History
The village was known as a pirate hold and was frequently raided by mercenaries hired by Amn and Baldur's Gate.

Rumors & Legends
One agent of the Harpers claimed the local tavern, the Broken Goblet, was run by mind flayers who used the establishment to gather information about illicit doings occurring around the Sword Coast.

Notable Locations
The Swordarm was an inn that once was a rundown place typical of pirates to frequent until a wizard, Aulyntar Cowlsar decided to renovate it using the life force of three of his apprentices—who could still be seen bound to the ceiling, their faces frozen in horror.

The Broken Goblet, was a tavern frequented by pirates and lawless folk where folks commonly entered with weapons drawn.

On Tarsakh 13 of the, roasted eel was served at Emriana Matrell's grand sixteenth birthday party, traditional grand event and coming of age celebration. ## Robes of the Dark Moon

Robes of the Dark Moon were enchanted robes worn by Beyl Van Yoren, a monk of the Dark Moon.

Powers
Enchantments weaved into these robes''' granted monks who wore them permanent speed similar to the haste spell. The enchantments had a downside. However, they made it harder for the wearer to concentrate.

History
Perhaps the most notable and the first wearer of a set of robes of the Dark Moon was Beyl Van Yoren. Like all monks in training at the Dark Moon order, Beyl was trained to use her speed over strength. Each training was a demand to move faster and faster. She found herself struggling with high demands until one day she started showing lightning speed. The young woman spent several months "trading favors" with a wizard to earn the magic robes of speed. Even though the Dark Moon tutors were satisfied, Beyl found herself unable to concentrate on lessons. Due to the break-neck speed, the young monk often got bored and restless. Eventually, Beyl Van Yoren abandoned her training and the robes and decided to move in with the wizard who created robes of the Dark Moon''.

By the late-14 century DR, several copies of these robes existed and they could be found along the Sword Coast as well as the city of Hillsfar in the Moonsea region.

## Rod of passage

Rods of passage were powerful enchanted tools that allowed its wielders to traverse the Prime Material and planes alike.

Powers
Rods of passage possessed several potent traversal magics. Each rod allowed the wielder to cast the following spells, one at a time: astral travel, dimension door, passwall, phase door, and teleport without error. These rods possessed a limited number of charges. However, each charge simply activated the rod of passage. Once activated, a rod could cast each of the listed spells once. The activation lasted for 24 hours, or until each spell was dispensed. If a wielder attempted to re-cast one of the rod's spells, it exhausted additional charge and reset all other spells, making them ready for use.

When a rod of passage used the astral travel function, it allowed for the transportation of up to five creatures, including the rod user. Upon activation of the astral travel function, rod could not be used again until the wielder returned from the Astral Plane.

Rods of passage radiated auras of the alteration and evocation magic. Recharging was extremely hard and only wizards of utmost arcane power could successfully accomplish that feat.

Notable Owners
Alustriel Silverhand was known to bear a rod of passage on occasion.
Shadeus, a Calishite thief and a member of the Twilight Blades in the late-14 century DR.
Vandemar Cordwin, General in service of Tempus from Arrabar in the Vilhon Reach circa 1370 DR.
Ysele the Cat, a half-elven wizard from Skullport circa the late-14 century DR.

## Root wine

Root wine was a type of an extremely potent dwarvish wine.

Description
Root Wine was renowned for its strength and palatability to dwarves. Another notable quality of the brew was its spoilage that turned it into poison. Despite becoming harmful and unfit for consumption, spoiled root wine still tasted good. Those who were unwise enough to drink poisonous root wine were rewarded with nausea and wracking pains.

Availability
Dwarven root wine was available during the old glory days of the elven city of Myth Drannor. The Master of the city's School of Wizardry, Azimer was known to consume the beverage.
On the Sword Coast, root wine was available in Neverwinter's Beached Leviathan tavern as of the late 15 century DR.

## Rowan Darkwood

Duke Rowan Darkwood was a nobleman from Oerth that arose as the factol of the Fated in the city of Sigil, the city considered to be the center of the Outer Planes.

Description
The Duke cut an imposing figure. He had a lean but athletic build and stood at  tall. He had many permanent scars that crossed his physique as well as his stern and weathered face. He was blind in one eye, a result of one such laceration.

Personality
While he was rather gruff and matter-of-fact when speaking to others, the Duke had a strong-will and stubbornness that could not be disregarded. He took great pride in his methodical and often-irritating manner in which he conducted business, regardless of how it was perceived by others.

For some reason, the Duke often claimed to be from the world of Toril.

Abilities
He had a number of innate protections to various magical spells, notable among them hold person, hypnotism, and ray of enfeeblement.

Activities
It was widely believed by those in Sigil that Duke Darkwood planned and schemed to take over the heart of Sigil from the Lady of Pain.

Possessions
The Duke possessed an array of enchanted items and weapons, including: a +2 giant slayer sword, a +5 ring of protection, a red ioun stone, a brooch of shielding, and carried with him a portable hole. He armored himself a suit of +3 splint mail, over which he wore a plain set of homespun clothes.

He regularly carried with him potions of hill giant control and growth.

History
Duke Darkwood's early life was one that could be expected of an Oerthian nobleman. He spent some time as an adventurer before settling down in his lands and starting a family. One fateful day, the Duke's sons accidentally summoned a cornugon named Ammagel by means of a magical deck of cards, and the devil attempted to claim them as his own slaves. Darkwood challenged Ammagel to a second game for the fate of his sons, and set down his own life as forfeit. The Duke won the game. His sons were set free but Darkwood was brought down to the Nine Hells and tortured for the next decade.

Rowan Darkwood's spirit proved indomitable. Ammagel tortured the mortal Duke for years but could not break his spirit. Eventually the malevolent cornugon came to respect his captive for his unshakable will and set him free. While Darkwood somehow managed to leave the Nine Hells of his own accord, he saw his family had moved on with their lives on Oerth, and he instead traveled across the planes.

He did not stay in one place long. Darkwood served as lieutenant to the deity Heimdall and even managed to save the plane of Ysgard from annihilation at some point. He was believed to have traveled to Vaasa in the Cold Lands region of Toril, during the 14 century as it was measured by the Faerûnian calendar.

Eventually the Duke traveled to Sigil in the Outlands and joined the faction known as the Fated. Within one year after his arrival, Duke Rowan Darkwood ascended as its factol. His rapid grab for power challenged the leaders of other factions, like Erin Montgomery of the Sensates.

Some time before the Faerûnian, a play entitled Comedy of Terrors penned by the Doomguard Raelis Shai was performed within the city of Sigil. The play was overtly critical of Duke Darkwood and considered quite defamatory to his character. In response, Duke Darkwood dispatched a band of extra-planar bounty hunters led by the tiefling Aawill to abduct Raelis' troupe. The bounty hunters manage to track down the blade Haer'Dalis to the city of Athkatla on the planet Toril.

Rumors & Legends
An individual matching the Duke's description was present during the Bloodstone Wars when Witch-King Zhengyi was deposed from his throne in Castle Perilous. This individual was said to have gone on to live as a lord and high priest in Vaasa. While Duke Darkwood did claim to have lived in Vaasa prior to his arrival in Sigil, he never confirmed any additional rumors about his involvements there.

## Road to Danger

Road to Danger is an adventure anthology for 2-edition Dungeons & Dragons featuring six short adventures reprinted from issues of Dungeon. Only one adventure, "The Inheritance", is set in the Forgotten Realms. A sequel anthology titled Dungeons of Despair was published in 1999.

## "Rock Eaters" They are Not

"Rock Eaters" They are Not! was a book dedicated to debunking perceived stereotypes about dwarves, found in the libraries of the Hand of the Seldarine before the stronghold's fall centuries before 1281 DR.

Content
The elven author of the book took upon themselves the task of researching many misconceptions and false beliefs held by the Fair Folk about dwarves. The research was performed after the author was invited to visit a dwarven citadel, Dorn's Deep. There they first witnessed dwarves' eating habits, which did not, contrary to popular belief, include eating dirt and rocks. The tome also praised Dorn's Deep's grand architecture as the author expected to be covered in dirt and soot during the visit, unbefitting a high elf. Friendly dwarves proudly demonstrated every wonder and beautiful detail of the underground realm to the fair visitor.

Despite being impressed with the stout folk of Dorn's Deep, the author, who hailed from the grand Hand of the Seldarine, still considered dwarves a "lesser race" and claimed that wonders created by the Tel-quessir were by far superior. However, the high elf did not feel comfortable pointing out dwarven inferiority during the tour.

History
"Rock Eaters" They are Not! was written and printed sometime between the citadel's construction circa 900 DR and its fall in the Battle of Seldarine's Hand. By the, the citadel was ruined and inhabited by ghostly apparitions of its former inhabitants. One copy could be found in the citadel's library curated by Custhantos, along with many other rare tomes.

## Rot troll

Rot trolls were a type of troll imbued with necrotic energy.

Description
A rot troll's flesh peeled off from the skin as soon as it formed, and the troll's body withered constantly.

Abilities
Unlike a normal troll, a rot troll eventually lost its ability to regenerate, but it still healed properly. The monster was pulsing with necrotic energy, which was emitted from its withering shape.

Combat
Due to a rot troll's rancid degeneration, standing too close to it exposed other creatures to its deadly emanations. Only acid or fire could stop this necrotic radiation.

Creation
During regeneration, a normal troll could become infected with waves of necrotic energy and form a symbiotic connection with that deathly force.

Habitats
Rot trolls could be found in the Mere of Dead Men on the Sword Coast North.

## Rod of flailing

A rod of flailing was a magic item that could be used as a weapon in combat.

Powers
These items gave off a hint of alteration magic under scrutiny from a detect magic spell.

When the command word was uttered the rod would transform into an enchanted double-headed flail. The flail would be an appropriate size for the wielder's current surroundings. Each of the flail's heads could be used to attack a target at the same time, effectively doubling the amount of potential harm from an attack. The heads could be used twice on a single target, or once each for two targets standing side by side.

Regardless of if the item was in rod or flail form, the wielder could expend a single charge to make it more difficult for opponents to strike them in combat. This magical effect lasted for ten minutes once activated.

Notable Owners
 Mathlas, a high priest of Bane.
 Rukn ud-Din Mahmud, a caravan guard captain in the Hordelands, carried a rod of flailing.

## Rope of entanglement

Ropes of entanglement were enchanted ropes used to restrain creatures.

Description
Ropes of entanglement appeared to be ordinary hempen ropes,  long and weighted between  and .

Powers
When a user of a rope uttered the item's command word while holding to its end, the rope of entanglement flew forward to entangle a single visible creature within  from the wielder. The target could avoid being restrained with quick enough reflexes, and if restrained, a strong creature could break free. If a rope sustained significant damage, it was destroyed.

Ropes of entanglement had several variations. Most differed in length, while others were unbreakable by her strength and could only be damaged by edged weapons. Longer variants or when used on smaller creatures, the ropes could entangle multiple targets.

When a  rope of entanglement was brought within  of a Methild's harp being played, its magics released any creatures restrained by the rope.

Creation
Ropes of entanglement could be created by capable crafters of wondrous items with the use of animate objects, animate rope, and entangle spells.

Reputation
Among the baatezu, erinyes carried ropes of entanglement to be used in battle.

Deity Khurgorbaeyag, a lieutenant of Maglubiyet, carried a  whip enchanted with magics of a rope of entanglement in his avatar form.

Notable Owners
Baelam "the Bold", an ex-thief from the Sword Coast who spent his wealth to replace his list hand with that of an automaton in the  late 14 century DR.
Desmonda, an erinyes who took a guise of perished Zhentil Keep agent in the  late 14 century DR.
Jaranatha Rylivvin, the proprietor of the Adamantine Mug in Athkatla in the late 14 century DR who kept a rope of entanglement behind a bar to deal with unruly customers.
Lauhren, a priestess of Lliira from Waterdeep, active in the late 14 century DR.
Lauren DeVillars, a noblewoman of Ravens Bluff and the leader of the oldest and wealthiest noble house in her city in the late 14 century DR.
Nurudin al-Kalaab, a member of the House of Reehan in Zakhara in the 14 century DR.
Shadowmoon Crystalembers, the Hierophant of the Emerald Enclave in the late 14 century DR.
Shadowsil, a ranking member of the Cult of the Dragon and an enforcer circa 1357 DR.
Shatagni, a bounty hunter from Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.
Verblen, the Slave Lord of Zhentil Keep and a ranking member of the Zhentarim in the late 14 century DR.

## Royal Ride

The Royal Ride was a wide street in the city of Suzail, Cormyr, that began at the easternmost part of the Promenade and was the location of the city's most wealthy noble villas and estates.

Description
The Royal Ride circled Castle Obarskyr's Royal Gardens to the north and ended reaching the front of the Marliir Mansion and the street that ran perpendicular to The Royal Ride and merged with the madly curving Court Close further south. The entire length of the Ride was dotted with the mansions of Suzail's most established and oldest families. The eastern end of the the street was the place of Suzail's most prominent temples, separated from the manors by the bridge over the Jaws of Lake Azoun.

Notable Locations
Shrines
 The Shrine of Malar stood halfway between the Jaws and the Promenade.
 The Shrine of Tempus was the easternmost structure that stood on the southeastern side of the crossing of Royal Ride and the Promenade.
 The Shrine to Tyr stood next door to the Shrine of Tempus.

Estates
 Alsevir Estate, the mansion of the Alsevir family. The mansion was the second building from the Ride's western end.
 Cormaeril Compound, the home of Cormaeril family of Cormyr. It stood across the Ride from the Greatgaunt Estate.
 Dauntinghorn House, the sprawling mansion home of the Dauntinghorn clan of Cormyr. The estate stood on the Royal Ride's southeastern end.
 Dracohorn Mansion, the estate of House Dracohorn that stood directly east from the Cormaeril Compound.
 Emmarsk Mansion, home of the Emmarsk family that stood to the east of the Huntcrown Estate.
 Goldfeather Estate, home of Goldfeathers of Suzail. The estate stood on the Royal Ride's northeastern end.
 Greatgaunt Estate, the home to the noble House Greatgaunt, and the former estate of House Wyvernspur. It was the closest building to the Jaws on the northern side of the Royal Ride.
 Huntcrown Estate, the manor of the House Huntcrown stood directly west of the Greatgaunt Estate.
 Huntsilver House, the manor of one of the oldest noble houses in Cormyr, the Huntsilvers. It stood between the Royal Gardens and the Dracohorn Mansion.
 Thistleflame Manor, more commonly known as the Thistleflame Keep, was the gated home of the noble Thistle family of Cormyr. The manor stood across the Royal Ride from the Shrine to Malar.

## Royalberry

Royalberry was a spell used by clerics of Sheela Peryroyl.

Effects
The caster casts this spell on a handful of berries. A single berry now provided the equivalent nourishment of a full meal. They also provided limited healing of wounds. If the individual was poisoned, the single berry acted in the same manner as slow poison. If more than three berries were consumed, they acted in the same manner as neutralize poison. If someone at six berries, they had the same effects as cure disease. Unlike the goodberry spell, this spell was not reversible.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Sheela Peryroyl and a handful of berries, which had to be freshly picked.

Appendix
References

---

## Rod of generalship

A rod of generalship was a rare enchanted rod that inspired soldiers.

Description
The rod measured  long and was made from pure gold. It was decoratd with valuable stones such as diamonds, rubies, and pearls.

Powers
When carried into battle and raised overhead by the wielder, the rod of generalship boosted friendly soldiers' combat skills within, making them more likely to successfully strike and harm enemies, while also making them more difficult to be hit themselves. The rod also boosted a soldier's morale.

The rod could only be used once per day but its effects lasted for 30—120 minutes.

History
Three of these rods were known to exist. They were favored by priests of Horus-Re, Anhur, and Assuran of the Three Thunders.

Inhabitants
Some 5,000 people lived in Rodanar in the, and 1,000 of these belonged to the garrison. Rodanar was led by a warrior named Hyurkes.

History
The war god Tchazzar founded Rodanar to serve as a fortress to thwart orc incursions from the Akanapeaks. Rodanar used to be a large city, but a war with Akanax during the 12 century DR left it in ruins. The new Rodanar that sprung up since was much smaller.

Over a thousand creatures could be cured by means of this spell before it expired. A creature could be healed by this spell once per week.

## Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild

The Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild was an association of roofers, slate-masons, shingle-cutters, thatchers, thatch-cutters, glaziers, sandglass makers, glass stainers of Cormyr.

Organization
The guild's insignia depicted three pink human hands on a red diamond-shaped background. The hand at the bottom reached towards the center of the diamond with its finger and thumb curling into an oval shape. Above the oval were the other two hands on both sides touching fingertips to form a shape of a roof.

Activities
Members of the Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild had a reputation of being the most easygoing of all the trades guilds of Cormyr. They were often late to the contracted jobs and tended to disappear for at least an entire day without deeming to notify of the absence or its reasoning. The same easygoing attitude also meant that members of the Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild were often available to be "day hires," working alone with a single assistant, precocious youth, and sluggards who had no experience in the trade. Although this was never the case for glaziers or glassworkers.

Base of Operations
Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild's headquarters was the Hardshutters House that stood on the northernmost end of Suzail's Market plaza, facing towards it on the crossing of Garth Street. The 14 century guildmaster custom-ordered a spell developed to magically animate glass shards that the guild members stored in the headquarters's attic. The shards were broken leftovers brought from numerous jobs performed by the guild. If an intruder appeared within the guild house or other trouble arose, the guildmaster opened the attic hatch and summoned the deadly whirlwind of glass with the use of a special enchanted finger-ring. This tornado of glass was similar to the effects of the blade barrier spell.

Members
As of the late 14 century DR, the guidmaster was Ullvor Mharivven, a male human rogue who appreciated finery and theatrics. He organizes exhibitions to improve the guild's reputation in Suzail and to jumpstart new fashionable trends in the industry. He was notorious for spreading rumors of ghost hauntings in order to buy, demolish and rebuild, or just renovate buildings.

Appendix
References

## Rod of bone

The rod of bone was a unique magic item wielded by Kelemvor, the God of the Dead.

The rod of bone possessed two powers, emitting one from each end of the rod. The first projected death, instantly killing any living target struck by the blast. Victims killed in this manner would levitate for several minutes in unbreakable stasis after their death. The target needed to be within Kelemvor's line of sight for this to function. The second power overwhelmed up to 80 different targets within a cube measuring  on each side. The death sleep caused victims to succumb to a coma in which their entire body ceased to function for one full day.

## Robe of the Watcher

The robe of the Watcher was a set of enchanted robes that originated from the drow city of Rilauven.

Description
These robes were bestowed upon whichever mage from Rilauven that demonstrated the greatest command over divination magic within the city's sorcerous academy.

Powers
The robe of the Watcher bolstered the wearer's physical defenses, their inherent magical resistance, and protected against spells cast against them. They also rendered the wearer nondetectible by magical means, and shielded them from the stupefying gaze of umber hulks.

Notable Owners
 Malavon Despana, archmage of Rilauven and patron of House Despana.

## Roq Silent Hunter

Roq Silent Hunter was a river-dwelling caiman katanga hunter from the jungles of the Malatran Plateau active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Roq was not particularly smart and the only topic he showed interest in was hunting.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Tribe of the Wise Ones became aware of a looming threat. The leader of the dreaded spider katanga, the Mother of a Thousand Young, was swollen with a thousand children ready to overflow the jungles. In preparation for the birthing, she initiated a ritual that sent deadly poisons into the rivers cascading off the Fire Mountain. Roq was among the younger and less-experienced heroes of the Living Jungle who answered the call. Silent Hunter's group was sent to enter the spider katanga caverns and faced hunting spider katangas who guarded previously captured adventurers.

It was unknown if Roq survived the battle, but the Mother of a Thousand Young was defeated, and heroes returned to the Village of the Wise Ones for their reward and facilitated the clean-up and nature's healing.

## Rod of favorable inclination

The rod of favorable inclination was a unique enchanted rod crafted by Laeral Silverhand for Vajra Safahr in the late 15 century DR. It was one of many pieces of so-called "Silverhand gear" crafted for adventurers who helped to protect Waterdeep.

Description
Silverhand crafted the hooked metal rod to represent Vajra's strong convictions, and to empower her existing gift for imposing her convictions and interests onto her adversaries to influence their actions in her favor.

Powers
The rod of favorable inclination allowed Vajra to dominate her enemies.

Notable Owners
 Vajra Safahr, the Blackstaff of Waterdeep as of the late 15 century DR, to whom Silverhand also gifted the seal of insight.

## Rod of the pact keeper

A rod of the pact keeper was a magic rod used by warlocks to empower their spellcasting.

Description
Rods suitable for warlocks swearing infernal pacts might be tipped by opals carved into the likeness of a clawed fist, and/or might have hafts decorated in the names of angels spelled backwards in the Infernal language. Meanwhile, rods suitable for warlocks swearing fealty to a Great Old One patron might be crafted from the tentacles of otherworldly aberrations and/or plague the wielder with nightmares and visions.

Powers
A rod of the pact keeper could be used to energize a warlock's offensive spells. About once each day, the wielder of a rod could also use it to regain a spent spell.

There existed rods of the pact keeper of varying levels of power, with more potent rods being commensurately rarer and more valuable.

History
Kuo-toa mystics in the Moonsea region were known to be buried with their rods of the pact keeper, which were notable for being coated in thin layers of slippery slime.

Powerful demons and devils were known to carry rods suitable for warlocks swearing infernal pacts.

Rods of the pact keeper could be found in Waterdeep as of the late 15 century DR.

Notable Owners
 Farideh, a tiefling warlock who worked as an adventurer on behalf of Laeral Silverhand, the Open Lord of Waterdeep, during the late 15 century DR. Her rod of the pactkeeper supercharged her eldritch blasts such that they could repel and push her enemies.
 SEER, a spymaster for the Lords' Alliance during the late 15 century DR, was in possession of a potent rod of the pact keeper which she would provide to her agents as needed.

In the, she was serving at the Herald's Horn tavern the night that Elminster Aumar purchased a wyrmtongue scepter from Surgath Ilder. She dropped a plate of hot tarts on her own toes at the sight of the wealth being traded in the tavern.

## Rod of Lathander

The rod of Lathander was a unique magic item that glowed with powerful daylight from sunrise to sunset.

Description
The rod was  long and had a tapered end that flattened into a serrated half-disc. The blades of the disc were embedded with rubies, rose-colored diamonds, and corundums. Removal of any of the gemstones would cause them to crumble into dust and the rod's magic would cease to function until they were replaced with a similar stone worth at least 5,000 gp.

Powers
Creatures within  of the shining rod of Lathander were protected by a death ward effect.

History
Commissioned by a paladin named, Dahaerlin of the Burning Brand, the rod of Lathander was created within the Spires of the Morning in the . The rod was wielded by Dahaerlin in the Shadow Plane while he rescued the lost villagers of Hespheira. Following Dahaerlin's death, the rod sat upon the altar of the Morninglord within the Spires of the Morning for over a century until the, when it was needed again to rescue Hespheira from shadow creatures. The rod returned to its place of honor until the, when it was stolen by thieves.

Rumors & Legends
Following its theft, a rumor persisted of a brightly illuminated cavern in Undermountain that led many of the Morninglord's faithful to believe it was the power of the rod of Lathander. Several expeditions ensued but none met with success in retrieving the rod.

## Roderio Thingoleir

Roderio Thingoleir was a wizard and alchemist who lived at Fourstaves House in Yhaunn.

Personality
Roderio was a quiet and thoughtful man, inclined towards research and analysis rather than flashy spells.

Abilities
Like the rest of his family, he used a staff to cast his spells. In addition to being a proficient wizard, he had a special passion for alchemy. He spent much of his time experimenting with alchemical preparations in his workshop at home.

His familiar was a brightly-striped green and blue lizard that lived in a warm terrarium.

Relationships
Roderio was the son of Strasus Thingoleir and Dagnalla Irongard, and the middle sibling of Malia and Keph.

He got along extremely poorly with his younger brother Keph, and they often sparred with each other verbally.

History
He grew up in Yhaunn at Fourstaves House and apprenticed under his parents to learn magic, becoming the titular fourth stave.

In Eleasis of 1373 DR, he participated in his family's study of The Leaves of One Night.

The same month, Keph sabotaged his alchemical workshop with magesbane dust, greatly underestimating its power. Instead of ruining a few hours work and embarrassing him, Roderio was almost killed as his entire workspace exploded, spraying him with acid and shattered glass that blinded him and melted his skin. Fortunately, his family came to the rescue and stabilized him with potions of healing until he could be healed by a temple.

Thanks to the treatment he received, within a few days his skin had healed, and within a tenday he was able to see again.

He later helped his family defend the Stiltways from a pack of shadow mastiffs summoned by Variance Amatick.

## Rond

Rond was a Reghedmen barbarian lout of Icewind Dale in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He wore leathers and furs.

History
In the late 1490s DR, Rond, Fennor, and Gurt were in Targos loading supplies onto a dog-pulled sled when Fennor spotted the Uthgardt Elk tribe barbarian Holga Kilgore and, from her tribal markings mistook her for a member of the Reghedmen Tribe of the Elk.The Honor Among Thieves novelization appears to mistake the Uthgardt Elk tribe of the Savage Frontier with the Reghedmen Tribe of the Elk of Icewind Dale. This article presumes it is Fennor who is mistaken, as he seems like the sort who would be. Coincidentally, Rond shares a name with a chief of the Uthgardt Elk tribe, suggesting they were intended to be the same. Rond knew Holga had been banished from her tribe from marrying a "Southlander", so Fennor said she should cover her markings to avoid angering the Tribe of the Elk. Soon, the gang were throwing mud and jeers at her, but Holga ignored them and left.

## Royappa

Royappa was a female rakshasa who got trapped inside the Tower of Ruin around 1359 DR.

Personality
Royappa was cunning and crafty, and a compulsive liar.

Possessions
She wore a amulet of proof against detection and location, and a jewelled necklace worth 4,000 gp.

Abilities
Royappa was able to command some divine and arcane powers, which mostly focused around disabling or tricking her enemies. As a rakshasa, she could shapechange into a more innocuous humanoid form.

History
In 1359 DR, she visited the Tower of Ruin to investigate the curious situation there, only to be trapped herself by the curse from Torm. Though she lived comfortably on the ground floor of the Tower, she could not leave or acquire Silveredge.

In the, she encountered adventurers who had entered the Tower in their own investigation. She presented herself as Elan, a human woman sent by Torm to protect the Tower. Given the chance, she would have tried to isolate and kill the adventurers.

## Robe of useful items

A robe of useful items was a magical robe that provided the wearer with handy tools and equipment at a moment's notice.

Powers
The robe was adorned with numerous small patches representing the items held by it, but only the wearer could see or use them. When needed, the wearer could detach a patch, upon which it would become a real and hopefully useful item.

A wide array of items could be produced from a robe of useful items. Every such robe was made with some of the following basic useful patches.
 A dagger.
 A lit bullseye lantern filled with oil.
 A 2-by-4-foot steel mirror.
 A  pole.
  of hempen rope.
 A large sack.

They were also made with a few more unusual patches.
 A pouch of 100 gp.
 A 1-foot long, 6-inch deep and wide silver coffer worth 500 gp.
 A self-fitting barred iron door up to  wide and tall that installed itself into a doorframe-shaped space.
 Ten gems worth 100 gp each.
 A  long wooden ladder.
 A living mule or horse with saddlebags.
 A  wide pit that affixed to the floor and opened up a void.
 Potions of healing, extra-healing, or cure serious wounds.
 A  long rowboat.
 A random lesser spell scroll.
 A pair of living war dogs.
 A 2-by-4-foot glass window that attached to a wall and opened a window frame through it.
 A portable ram.

## Roaringcrest

Roaringcrest was a ruined keep once used by pirates on the isle of Raerest in Sea of Swords, just off the shores of Candlekeep.

Location
Roaringcrest built on a natural harbor on Raerest's southeastern side, on the southern shores of the island's freshwater lake chain.

Structure
By the late 14 century DR, Roaringcrest was a ruin reclaimed by vegetation.

History
Long time before the late 14 century DR, Roaringcrest was a pirate stronghold, until its destruction at the hands of the Twilight Three, a trio of liches from the isle of Thelve. The liches pelted the keep with spells that turned it into an area of wild magic. After the pirate occupants, the ruins were home to crimson deaths, darktentacles and automatons. Even by the late 14 century DR, pirates of the Sea of Swords told stories of ancient buccaneer treasures hidden within Roaringcrest.

Thardock, who was an officer in the Purple Dragons, carried a rod of cancellation.

Rope of constriction was a type of magical rope.

A rope of constriction resembled a rope of climbing or rope of entanglement.

The rope continued to strangle its victims until a dispel magic spell was cast on it. Those entwined in the rope were unable to cast spells or break free by any means.

## Rold

Rold was a guard in Shadowdale as of the mid-14 century DR.

Description
He had a mostache.

Personality
He had great calm and presence of mind even in the most awkward moments. He was open about finding the state of being on the offensive to be easier on his temper than being a guardsman.

Relationships
He was the uncle of Naera, a fact he preferred not to bring up around Torm. He had great respect for the adventurer.

History
He claimed to have seen something of the road Torm (of the Knights of Myth Drannor) walked, strongly implying he had been an adventurer.

In the, he was the one to take Torm from the arms of a wench, his niece Naera, to keep guard on the spellfire wielder Shandril Shessair, then a guest of the castle. Later, he was the one to save Shandril from a fellow guard, Culthar, then in the employ of the Cult of the Dragon. The next morning, he was relieved Shandril wasn't staying at the castle longer.

Per some early reports, Rold was also a guard during the tests that Elminster did on spellfire. Those were not repeated in more definitive reports.Rold is not named during spellfire tests in the 2005 reissue.

The Rose-Crowned Tower was small.

Ronger Delphos's recruits were armed with magic wands that dispensed a hold person spell. To counter this, Waterdeep's thieves' guilds purchased many belts of agility. ## Royal Docks (Port Nyanzaru)

The Royal Docks of Port Nyanzaru in Chult were an enclosed section of the city's Harbor Ward that contained docks reserved for the exclusive use of the city's merchant princes or their honored guests.

Description
The Royal Docks were only used by the merchant princes or those whom they wished to impress, generally meaning foreign dignitaries, ambassadors, or other powerful merchants.

The name "Royal Docks" was derived from a time when Chult had been ruled by literal kings and queens as opposed to Merchant Princes.

## Rokh Manti

Rokh Manti was a warlock and artificer active in Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR. During the events of the death curse, he was hired by the Harpers to investigate the curse's cause.

Description
He was an inventor and crafter of magical contraptions and devices, including traps, tools, vehicles, and even his own body. Virtually the only part of his body that was still original was his brain, with the rest being a construction of his own design. His brain was protected within a brass skull that was equipped with an emergency escape device that could gate it away to a back-up body if needed.

Relationships
Rokh Manti was on good terms with the Harpers.

He was known to have dealings with the Shadow Thieves of Neverwinter, such as for acquiring magic items needed to build his inventions.

His patron was the enigmatic artificer known as Kwalish.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, Rokh Manti had designed several devices for the cleric Alfin Biscop, including a trapped lock mechanism for his vault (made by imprisoning imps and mephits inside of glass chess pieces) and a telescope for studying the planes (made from still-living rogue modrons).

Several months into the events of the death curse, at the demand of his patron and with funding from the Harpers, Rokh was tasked with traveling to the Positive Energy plane to investigate claims made by Alfin Biscop and other Harper contacts (including Szass Tam) that the death curse might have been caused by a disruption of the flow of positive energy to Toril. To make this trek, he began construction on a vehicle of his own design intended to travel to the Positive Energy plane. This vehicle was to be made of brass and to resemble a xag-ya (consisting of a small, spherical cockpit with long tentacles) with "eyes" made of a honeycombed arrangement of gems of seeing to allow him a perfect ability to see into the hazardous plane. The craft was also equipped with the ability to emit pulses of negative energy as a defense mechanism.While not stated explicitly, Rokh Manti's vehicle is implied to be a variation on the apparatus of Kwalish. Rokh made use of his Shadow Thieves contacts to acquire the gems of seeing for this project, and while doing so, he let slip to them that the lock which he had made for Alfin Biscop's vault involved a life-or-death game of chess. The Shadow Thieves used this information to plan a heist of Biscop's mansion, and sent in a skilled chess player named Pion.

Rokh ultimately made use of his brass craft to travel to the Positive Energy plane, and he discovered that all of Toril's positive energy was being siphoned directly to Chult. His craft was destroyed soon after this discovery by a group of xag-ya overwhelming it with positive energy, but his brain managed to gate back to the Prime Material plane just in time, allowing him to report back with what he had learned.

## Rojak Hammersmith

Rojak Hammersmith was the dwarven co-owner of The Baker Boys bakery in the Merchant's District of Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Rojak was an atypical dwarf. He shaved his beard and only had a large handlebar mustache. Unlike many of his kin, he was also was fun-loving, boisterous fellow who did not consume any alcohol. Both men were kind, happy-go-lucky, and friendly, often breaking out into impromptu food-fighting.

Possessions
Rojak Hammersmith and his partner, the prankster hin named Culwen Furryfeet, ran The Baker Boys bakery on 1 Curios Circle, just off Merchant's Way. The hop stood on a small cul-de-sac, with Ephram's Antiquities being its next-door neighbor. Their bakery produced fresh pastries, pies, and donuts daily.

History
In the, many smaller businesses were targeted by a group of criminals demanding protection money. To make things even more preposterous, these bandits wore the faces of renowned heroes and adventurers of the Living City. These criminals, Marella, Elizza, Moamar, and Rundell, were members of the warlord Myrkyssa Jelan's army that waged war on the Vast just a year prior. They used a magic wand that let them take on other people's guises. The criminals stroke at The Baker Boys and the rest of the shops in its cul-de-sac. Their extortion resulted in Malishar's Magicks being a burnt-out husk.

The Baker Boys lost 500 gold ravens paid for "protection" and 300 more in destroyed merchandise. Despite the loss, Culwen and Rojak remained loud and happy, aiming to survive the best they could. Two days later, a group of heroes, who seemingly just did the extortion, returned in the middle of a pie-fight, narrowly escaping the fruit-filled humiliation to their faces. It took some convincing, but Culwen and Rojak believed them to be real individuals who were investigating extortion. Instantly, Bakers warmed up to the adventurers and happily treated them to some of their freshest pastries. Rojak Hammersmith's interview gave the adventurers an invaluable clue that would eventually lead them to finding the culprits. The dwarf remembered that the thugs were carrying mining tools and wore miners' boots, as well as they had a distinct smell of underground upon them.

## Rosymorn Monastery

Rosymorn Monastery was a temple of Lathander found in the Western Heartlands. As of the late 15 century DR it had been abandoned by the Lathanderian clergy, but claimed by a contingent of githyanki. The gith referred to the underground reaches of the monastery as Crèche Y'llek.

Location
The monastery was situated within a cliffside near the Sword Coast, north of the River Chionthar and east of Baldur's Gate.

Structure

The monastery comprised four prominent structures constructed around a central courtyard. Beneath the structures was an network of chambers and hallways. It was within these halls that Rosymorn's monks conducted the business of the Lathanderian faith in their quarter of the Heartlands.

Interior
Befitting a temple dedicated to the god of light and creativity, Rosymorn boasted grand stained-glass windows, finely-sculpted statues dedicated to the Morninglord, and ground stone murals depicting his holy symbol.

The western structure housed the monastery's winery, where they produced and housed casks and casks of firewine.

The northern structure of the monastery featured exquisite architecture and beauteous decor. Set within its walls were marble columns, and stained glass windows that overlooked an elaborate stained glass mosaic of the Dawnmaster's crest set upon the floor. The large mosaic depicted the monastery's four of devoted Dawnmasters: Seed, Stockhold, Vaseid, and Welkinglory, each wielding their favored weapons of war.

The underground tunnels of Rosymorn housed the monastery's infirmary, storage rooms, private chambers, the offices of the Dawnmaster, and a grand audience chamber. They had etched into their walls an ancient Netherese sigil circle.

Services

While the temple was in service, it remained a beacon to all of the Lathanderian faith. Any who wished to make the trek up the mountainside pass could witness his glory by admiring the most-celebrated relic that was proudly displayed within.

Defenses

Rosymorn monastery was one of the most well-protected temples dedicated to the Morninglord. Its courtyard housed a large mechanical device that resembled a cross between a crystalline telescope and an armillary sphere. In truth, it was a powerful weapon—referred to as the "Solar Lance"―that harnessed the the power of the sun. The weapon served as a failsafe contingency plan: if anyone unworthy attempted to steal the treasure of Rosymorn, the weapon would be used to collapse the temple in on itself.

The power source for the temple's weapon was set within its innermost depths. Four Lathander's solar machines―giant crystals that could store and emit solar energy―fed their energy into a highly-guarded, underground pedestal that housed temple's true secret treasure: blood that fell from Lathander's mortal avatar.

History

At some point a contingent of githyanki led by M'lar Rih'al, mounted atop red dragons, assailed Rosymorn from on high. Some monks attempted to defend themselves, while others fled for their lives. Yet other venerated monks successfully sealed off the deepest passageways of the monastery; Dawnmaster Vaseid managed to seal away with magic the temple's greatest treasure in a vault deep within the heart of the mountain.

Ultimately, the assaulting githyanki prevailed. They established within the underground halls of Rosymorn a gith crèche: a hatchery away in the Prime―away from the agelessness of the Astral Plane―within which their eggs could mature, and newborn githyanki could age into their juvenile years. Birthing pools were established within, the monk's infirmary was retrofitted into an illithid experimentory, complete with a zaith'isk. This otherworldly device crafted from flesh and metal that could be used to "purify" illithid tadpoles and anyone they infected.

Some time later, the gith of crèche Y'llek were redirected to achieve new goal's according to Vlaakith's will. Ch'r'ai W'wargaz led the gith inquisition to locate and retrieve the Astral Prism, in order to destroy the elder brain and mind flayer colony beneath Moonrise Towers. His followers established an audience room and set within it a planecaster, a device capable of granting travel to the Astral Plane, at a destination atop the corpse of a dead god.

Rumors & Legends
Rosymorn was the site that boasted one of greatest Lathanderian relics, the Blood of Lathander. Those four drops of the god's blood―which had spilled during his battle with Mystra's chosen Sammaster―remained suspended within amber and set within an enchanted mace. The weapon was considered one a relic among those of greatest importance to the Lathanderian faith. It was so highly-protected, the temple itself could be self-destructed if anyone but one of Lathander's dawnmasters attempted to retrieve it from its resting place.

The Blood of Lathander had previously been stolen by Sharran agents, but retrieved by Lathander's faithful during a conflict between the two groups near the River Chionthar. Then-paladin Vaseid took charge in its retrieval. His followers descended upon the Sharrans, but the amber containing the the blood was damaged in the conflict. Deeming it too unstable to return to Hap, Vaseid instead built a small chapel around it and declared it would one day become a great monastery, marking the founding of Rosymorn.

Inhabitants
After it was abandoned, Rosymorn became home to wild animals, packs of monstrous creatures, and most notably, a contingent of githyanki. Throughout its history, packs of gremishka infested the temple, while at one point pack of kobolds raided the firewine cellars. A flock of giant eagles, including one of ancient age, formed a nest in the monastery's central courtyard.

While it was not technically intelligent, a single bust in Rosymorn had been enchanted with a magic mouth spell.

A single conjured guardian of faith roamed the halls of the abandoned temple, having been summoned during the gith assault.

Notable Inhabitants
 Novice Peran, a Lathanderian monk who had a mishap with a statue of the Morninglord.
 Ghustil Stornugoss, a githyanki scholar and physician.
 Kith'rak Therezzyn, commander of Creche Y'llek.
 Xavier, the giant eagle hatchling.
 Zel'i, a rather sadistic gith juvenile.

## Rohav Steelsar

Prince Rohav Steelsar "the Exile" was an ill-fated child of the exiled Amnian Parhek dynasty.

Relationships
Rohav was the second son of Princess Rasa Parhek Dornar Steelsar, and was the sibling of Choris, Rosa, and Rhothar.

History
Rohav was born in Silverymoon, and was only 8 years old in the  when the family was found and attacked by Amnian assassins. Dornar and Choris fought to the death, Rosa was killed, and Rasa and Rhothar managed to escape, but Rohav was kidnapped and taken away. He was brainwashed by his captors and led to believe they had actually rescued him, and lived in Baldur's Gate while plotting to take the throne for himself. In 1305 DR he betrayed his uncle Prince Malek the Coinmage, leading to his death and the deaths of his three children. Rohav was poisoned to death by his vizier and controller Arlen Bormul in 1306 DR, putting an end to the last known heir of the Parhek dynasty.

## Roa

Lady Roa was a thrice-betrayed noblewoman, the namesake of The Shackles of Roa, who was martyred long before the early 14 century DR.

Description
During the last part of her life, Lady Roa was a petite and willowy sixteen-year-old girl.

History
Long before the 14 century DR, Lady Roa was born into a noble family to a cruel and opportunistic lord. Not even sixteen winters old, Roa was forced into a marriage to an abusive nobleman, Duke Sorim. Roa's father knew perfectly well his daughter would be tormented by her spouse, but the marriage benefited him politically. Such was the first betrayal Lady Roa suffered.

The second betrayal came from her new husband. Duke Sorim sent assassins among Roa's handmaidens when the Lady visited her beloved brother, Baron Remin. Roa's brother was murdered in his sleep, and his lands passed to Duke Sorim. The third betrayal suffered by Lady Roa came from her sister, Lady Rana. The sister longed for Duke Sorim's hand in marriage, his power, and the crown. Lady Rana conspired with Sorim to end the king's life and accuse his rightful heir of being a bastard born from young Roa. Ruler-less, the crown would then be passed to Duke Sorim and his new Queen, Lady Rana.

Still a youth, Lady Roa was shackled with a ball-and-chain and tossed into Duke Sorim's dungeons until the plot to overthrow the king reached its fruition. Roa's cries and moans echoed across her husband's keep for many days. Her laments annoyed the Duke so much that he sent a Ducal Guard member to batter the petite girl into silence. The guard hit the woman across the face and, when she fell, tried to kick Roa in the stomach. But, swift as lightning, Lady Roa pounced up and strangled the Ducal Guard with her chains.

Now free, Lady Roa wielded her ball-and-chain as a weapon and burst into Sorim's court. Her attack was relentless; not even the Ducal Guard's bolts slowed her down. When the young woman reached Sorim's throne, she broke his best bodyguard like a twig and bludgeoned her cruel husband with ungodly strength. The Duke's thrice-enchanted breastplate forged out of meteoric iron could not protect him from the ball-and-chain. It broke the man's ribs, perforating his lungs and heart, instantly killing him.

Following this, records of the thrice-betrayed Lady differed. Some said that the young woman died moments after ending the Duke's life, succumbing to many a bolt that pierced her tiny frame. Others claimed that Lady Roa was killed by Rana's dagger. Some romantics even claimed that Lady Roa survived and lived out the rest of her life in peace, happily.

By 1312 DR, the woman's story was remembered through The Shackles of Roa, the ball-and-chain that Roa used for revenge. Sometime after the story's end, the The Shackles of Roa were altered, looped, and affixed together so the ball-and-chain could be used as a powerful enchanted flail. The ball bore etchings that read "In Righteousness Lies My Strength." The Shackles' last recorded location was a tomb underneath the Black Raven Monastery, in the mountains atop the Ice Lakes in northwest Faerûn.

History
In the, Bowser of Ravens Bluff attended the year-end celebration festival in the town of Mossbridges in the Vast. The alesmith took his first attempt at winemaking – Dark Maiden Wine, to the wine-tasting and grading competition. Even though the completion rules dictated Bowser could only bring one variety of wine, they said nothing about ale, so the dwarf brought all twelve types of ales of his making, including Rock Strained Ale.

## Road of Jackals

The Road of Jackals was a pass through the Scimitar Spires, known to the ancient Netherese as Humaithira.

Geography
The Road was one of two passes that cut through the Scimitar Spires, along with the Gap of Skulls. It was located towards the southern end of the Scimitar Spires, and could shorten a journey past the mountains by at least a hundred miles.

History
Humaithira served as the location of the Last Stand at Humaithira in, which saw several hundred Netherese soldiers led by Strategor Matick fight against the giants of Thane Blóta. Though Matick was slain and the pass taken by the giants, the heroic sacrifice allowed a later Netherese army to ultimately defeat the army of giants.

By the 14 century DR, few Bedine had explored the pass, and thus not many could make their way through the Road of Jackals.

At some point around,The default 2-edition date of 1368 DR is assumed. a number of caravans went missing trying to find another way across the mountains.
