## Role Playing Game Association

The Role Playing Game Association, also known as RPGA, was an organized play association founded in 1980, most notably known for it's Living City program.

History
RPGA was originally founded by TSR, Inc. as a way to promote their products by creating a "club for role-playing gamers". The person assigned to this task and the eventual founder of the association, was Frank Mentzer, one of TSR's first full-time employees. Among the systems used in the first organized plays was AD&D.

The plays proved to be a very popular concept with the RPG community. The longest running one was Living City (originally started in 1987), while the one that attracted the largest amount of players over it's lifespan was Living Greyhawk.

The "living campaigns" organized by the association were the first of its kind, and the format of the universe being shared between all the players in different sessions became the main inspiration for MMORPG games.

In 2010, RPGA began to run the D&D Encounters program, which later was continued by the Adventurers League, which effectively replaced the Role Playing Game Association in 2014.

List of Programs
The following programs ran by RPGA took place in the Realms:

 Living City
 Living Forgotten Realms
 Sarbreenar the Living City
 Legacy of the Green Regent
 Living Death
 Living Jungle
 D&D Encounters

## Roldro Tattershar

Roldro Tattershar was a bard and Harper operating in Suzail and Marsember in Cormyr during the late 14 century DR.

Description
Roldro wore gray, dusty, and faded leathers during undercover stints, and he had a "villainous" fake mustache to go with it. Normally, he was dressed in flamboyantly florid tones.

Relationships
He had been a guest of Lady Joysil Ambrur. They were close enough that he called her by a different name, "Ammaratha", and knew enough to give her room when he believed she'd transform into a dragon.

History
In the, Roldro Tattershar was drinking at the Green Wyvern in Suzail when two men teleported in front of him. Those men, the Red Wizard Harnrim Starangh and War Wizard Huldyl Rauthur, discussed briefly the plans of Vangerdahast, the then-retired Royal Mage of Cormyr, to bind dragons to service. They teleported away shortly after; as they planned, Roldro overheard them and immediately used a portal hidden in the tavern to head over to Marsember and share this information with Lady Joysil Ambrur, with the nefarious intent of "stirring the cauldron".

Once at the residence of the lady, he was stopped, first by the guard Melarvyn, and then by the steward, Elward. Both refused him entry; removing his mustache, he threatened Elward to tell the watch about a certain Elward of Westgate. The man relented; when Roldro finally was face-to-face with Lady Joysil, she instructed Elward to move as far back as the southern pond. Roldro knew her by the name of Ammaratha; he gave her space, taking several steps back, as he revealed to her that he'd overheard two War Wizards of Cormyr revealing that Vangerdahast was binding dragons. This turned out to be the right move, as she immediately turned into her natural form as a song dragon and flew straight through the ceiling.

He took a chest of jewels as a down-payment, and departed through a secret passage, anticipating the steward would be furious.

## Rowboat Shrine

The Rowboat Shrine was a shrine found in Miyama Province in Kozakura during the mid–14 century.
Description
Situated along the shores of Nora-ko, this shrine was a modest stone memorial erected by the villagers of Ibara. It commemorated the site of the clandestine meetings between two lovers whose story was woven into the fabric of local lore.

History
According to legend, on a night fraught with a fearsome storm, the man vanished while trying to reunite with his beloved. The shattered remnants of his boat later drifted ashore at the very spot where she awaited his arrival. Overwhelmed by grief, she eventually succumbed to her sorrow and passed away.

In the wake of this tragedy, it was rumored that the spirits of the lovers continued to linger, forever parted by the waters of Nora-ko. He was often envisioned rowing his boat, while she was pictured standing on the shore, each unable to reach the other. The local sandpipers were said to possess a distinctive call that eerily resembled the mournful utterance of his name.

## Rotvine defiler

A rotvine defiler was an abomination born from a divinity of fertility.

Description
These creatures were described as a morass of tendrils, vines, rotting animals, and mud. In one particular case, a rotvine defiler resembled a treelike, ovoid creature made of indistinct flesh, with four flat legs and a single horn akin to that of a unicorn; the horn measured, while the creature was twice that on a side.

Personality
Their intentions were generally limited to the destruction of all life.

Combat
The despoiled morass of a rotvine defiler caused an aura that extended to about  away; anyone standing within would be slowed by it. All their attacks caused bodily withering to any living creature they struck. They generally attacked with lashing tendrils, which made it nigh-impossible to move anywhere for those they managed to strike. Sometimes, their tendrils struck out with rot, becoming a hazard for anyone standing close; those who became restrained by the tentacles of rot were soon struck with earthly maws that continued to break down the bodies of those who had yet to free themselves.

In response to severe damage, rotvine defilers released an explosion of vile vapors that caused even more intense, deadly withering to anyone within . Those who actually managed to defeat the defiler would induce another such burst.

Society
Rotvine defilers were sometimes accompanied by blood fiends, when they allowed other creatures to accompany them at all. They generally remained encysted until a sacrifice released their seals.

In one particular case, a rotvine defiler was bred by the Rotting Man, a Talonite patriarch of the Unapproachable East. The creature remained encysted until commanded to reveal itself by him in 1373 DR.

History
In the, the Rotting Man of Dun-Tharos attempted to strike down Araluen, an aspect of Lurue, with a creature born of the corrupted majority of her essence that he'd stolen a decade prior: a rotvine defiler named simply the Step-Daughter of Talona. Unfortunately for him, Araluen was able to assimilate this essence again and proceeded to run him out of his stronghold with it.

Notable Rotvine Defilers
 AraluenWhile neither Araluen nor the Step-Daughter of Talona are described as rotvine defilers in Lady of Poison, nor are they a perfect match for the description of one, the novel is written by Bruce Cordell, one of the lead writers of Open Grave, which presents the rotvine defiler. The Step-Daughter is also described as an abomination, as are the rotvine defilers, and her backstory echoes many such aspects: she is created from the corrupted essence of a deity of the wild and greenery. Thus, it is assumed in this article that the rotvine defiler is intended to be the same kind of creature as the Step-Daughter of Talona.

## Rondras Stormserpent

Rondras Stormserpent was a heir of House Stormserpent in the early mid-15 century DR.

Relationships
He and Marlin Stormserpent hated each other a lot. He was a heir of House Stormserpent.

History
Rondras Stormserpent was one of the four Stormserpent heirs in the 1430s DR. When Nethglas Stormserpent stole the Wyverntongue Chalice, he told his siblings, before deciding he'd prefer no one knew it and swearing them to secrecy on pain of death. After Nethglas killed his brother Elgrym Stormserpent for sharing this information with Nael Rowanmantle, only two of his siblings were left alive knowing of the prank.

When in, Emvar Obarskyr was slain in the Vast Swamp close to Sembia, Nethglas Stormserpent was killed with him. While his body was being brought to the house, Marlin Stormserpent quietly poisoned Rondras, killing him and becoming the only heir.

Personality

Relationships
He was the eldest son of Velyandra Illance and Daeclander Illance, and had stretched the man's patience as far as it would go. Rothglar had eight brothers, per Arclath Delcastle. He had made a rival of Marlin Stormserpent. His bodyguard was named Marlazander the Mighty.

History
Rothglar attended a feast in early Mirtul of the, in which he was seen by Amarune Whitewave listening close into some private converse.

Before the night when Marlin Stormserpent retrieved the Wyverntongue Chalice from the Royal Palace of Suzail, he'd bribed Verrin, one of Marlin's servants, and the disloyal man waited for Marlin at the end of the passage the latter used to get out of the castle. Marlazander was there with him; they had not unlocked the gate and were waiting for him. Marlin killed them too quickly, even from behind the bars, to learn what their intentions had been.

There was a rumor, actually a manipulation by Marlin Stormserpent, that Rothglar, alongside Harmond Hawklin and Seszgar Huntcrown, were planning to unleash a some sort of "ball of Spellplague" from a chest during the Council of the Dragon.

Interior
Atmosphere
The room was seldom used; it had a number of soft, stuffed beds of matched hues and many crimson draperies. There was a portrait of King Duar, almost certainly Duar Obarskyr, with cut-out eyes that allowed vigilance of the room from within a hidden passage.

Defenses
The passage contained a trap at some point, which had since claimed a War Wizard.

History
Forty years prior to, the room had been popular for trysts amongst courtiers; at that point the War Wizards took exclusive possession of the key.

Before the Council of the Dragon in 1479 DR, the ghost of Regent Alusair Obarskyr and the fugitive wizard Elminster Aumar used a passage around the room while sneaking through Castle Obarskyr, and briefly spied inside. There, they saw the War Wizard Raereene and the palace server Kreane in the midst of a tryst. The escapees stopped to look only briefly.

## Roald Tunnelfist

Roald Tunnelfist was a dwarf from Clan Battlehammer, residing in Dwarven Valley of Kelvin's Cairn in the late 13 century DR.

Description
Roald Tunnelfist was a stout dwarven man with a grayish skin tone and light brown beard and hair. He wore a set of enchanted +2 chainmail and carried a magical +2 defender battleaxe.

History
In the, Roald Tunnelfist and several other dwarves were tasked with scouting the slopes of Kelvin's Cairn, southeast of the town of Lonelywood, to keep an eye on a suspicious congregation of the northern barbarians –  a historic threat to the Ten Towns and the dwarves of the Valley. Roald's gand was spotted, and they were attacked on sight. The barbarians gave chase, cornering the dwarves against a bluff. Roald and his group decided to fight and die and hopefully take some of the barbarians with them. Roald Tunnelfist was the last dwarf standing, surrounded by the bodies of his comrades. Suddenly, the barbarians stepped aside, allowing a giant of a man to step forward. The Reghedmen "king" who led the gathered tribes – Wylfdene, stepped forward, bearing a giant double-bladed axe. The king attacked, Roald barely had a chance of blocking the deadly blow with his shield. Under the barbarian's relenting strength, the shield shattered, and Roald plummeted off the bluff.

Sometime later, Roald Tunnelfist came to it, surrounded by a mound of broken branches that slowed his fall. Badly hurt, the dwarf limped into the closest settlement – the town of Lonelywood. Since then, he has been staying at The Whistling Gallows, licking his wounds, and even getting to meet the famed Heroes of Icewind Dale, who were called to mitigate the barbarian threat. Roald shared the story of his encounter and pointed the heroes to where the barbarian camp was established, east of Lonelywood. He would eventually make his way back to Clan Battlehammer and update the elders on the looming threat. and the loss of dwarven lives.

## Rocking mount

A rocking mount, also commonly known as a rocking horse, was a wooden child's toy made to resemble some form of mount and braced upon two curved bands similar to those of a rocking chair.

Description
Crafted from wood, these toys could be crafted to resemble a variety of different mounts, though most commonly resembled horses.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold rocking mounts imported from Cormyr that they advertised as being perfect for "practicing knight-errants". These came in a wide variety of styles, ranging from the mundane horse (destrier) to pegasi and unicorns, to more strange mounts such as sea lions or various colors of dragons. All were sold at the same bargain price of 4 gold pieces.
 The Toysmiths, a toyshop in Ravens Bluff that was owned and operated by the gnome brothers Gunder and Relvan Gaewilder, sold rocking mounts in many of the same styles as Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue. However, the brothers charged different prices for each style of mount. Standard horses were the cheapest at 3 gp, pegasi and unicorns sold for 4 gp, sea lions for 5 gp, and dragon styled rocking mounts for 8 gp. Additionally, Toysmiths offered rocking mounts styled to resemble griffins at a cost of 4 gp.

Notable Owners
 The Cassalanter family owned and kept a rocking mount shaped like a warhorse within their villa's playroom in the late 15 century DR.
 The treasure hoard of Guh inside Grudd Haug included a rocking horse with silver inlays and blue quartzes for eyes.
 The Martikov family, operators of the Wizard of Wines in Barovia, owned a rocking horse that was painted to resemble a nightmare.
 The Shadowdusk family owned a rocking horse, that at one point had been partially scorched by fire, and kept it within their family's vault of treasures.
 Skabatha Nightshade owned a rocking horse she flew upon. But rather than being crafted from wood, her rocking horse was a unicorn named Elidon that had been transformed into that form by the Hourglass Coven and its horn removed.

Rorn was one of the bullyblades protecting Traevyn Illance when the latter met with Morligul Downdagger, who had just captured Rauligus Helderstone (actually Mirt the Moneylender) for him. ## Royal Gorget of Battle

The Royal Gorget of Battle was an enchanted gorget in possession of the royal family of Cormyr.

The powers, while nondescript, were largely defensive in nature.

History
The Gorget was loaned by Elminster Aumar, wizard and Chosen of Mystra, to King Palaghard Obarskyr I of Cormyr before his reign to ensure his safety. Palaghard in turn let it be worn by his wife Enchara, with Elminster's blessing. Afterwards, it was left in the Warhorn Room, displayed as a state treasure of Cormyr.

On Mirtul 5 of the, Elminster stole the Gorget from its display case, unaware that Vainrence had cast a tracing spell on it. He then fed the energies of the Gorget to the Simbul, who required magical energies from items periodically to remain sane, as a result of the Spellplague; this destroyed the device. The tracing spell eventually drew a force of mages bearing orders to retrieve the Gorget or utterly annihilate Elminster; they persisted in this endeavor upon catching up with him, despite his protestations.

They were ambushed on their first night out of Starmantle by a chiming mist, the Slayer. Rolian did notice the sound, but when he warned Paeregur, the latter moved to the edge of the cliff where they'd stopped to check up on Arvas. ## Rockstinger

Rockstingers were fish known for being highly poisonous.

Description
They were said to be very ugly.

Ecology
The poison of the rockstinger was a highly effective defense against predators.

Uses
Naturally, rockstinger poison was a valuable and terrible tool for assassins. Even a light nick from a dagger coated with the poison caused agonizing, scream-inducing pain like fire in the victim's veins. The effect would then evolve to cause painful muscle contractions that led to copious vomiting. Even after the acute effects of the poison wore off, the victim felt aches and fatigue for days afterward.

History
During the mid–15 century DR, the assassin Barrabus the Gray made use of rockstinger poison while working for Herzgo Alegni. He used the poison to intimidate Lord Hugo Babris of Neverwinter, going so far as to inflict the poison on a helpless guard just to instill fear of its terrible effects. The effects of the poison were so terrible that this demonstration nearly elicited regret from the ruthless Barrabus.

## Roadwatch bow

The roadwatch bow was an old magical longbow found in the ruins of Beckside Castle, a ruin in Cormyr.

Powers
The bow was strongly axiomatic and aligned with law, giving it great power against chaotic beings. It also unerringly aimed for weak points and vitals in the target, making it exceptionally lethal. The enchantments on the longbow meant that it remained in perfect condition despite being lost for so long.

History
The roadwatch bow was once used by Purple Dragons that patrolled and watched over the High Road, only to be lost in the ruins of Beckside Castle when it was destroyed during the Goblin War of the last century. It was discovered again over a century later in the mid-1480s DR when Harper agents fought the Netherese arcanist Gaston Wyrst for a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll hidden there. At the time, the old bow was hidden inside the castle's remaining tower, which had recently served as a signal beacon until an umbral gargoyle laired there.

## Roasted turkey

Roasted turky was a fowl dish made from a sizable turkey found in some areas of Faerûn Maztica, and the Malatran Plateau.

Availability
Baldurian turkey roasts were notably flavored with red wines, oranges, and grapes, giving the succulent dish a complex and sweet taste.

In Archendale, roasted turkey was a staple of The Long Night festival, where one could see guests brandishing turkey legs as an entire meal.

In Asbravn in the Sunset Vale, roasted turkey could be found on the menu of The Board Laid Bare inn. Roasted turkey there was an eveningfeast staple and served in gravy.

The metropolis of Ravens Bluff in the Vast served turkey roasts in some of its eateries, including the Shod Tallon and the Sunflower Inn, where a serving of such roasted bird was sold for five silver pieces.

The roasted wild turkey was a dish invented on the Malatran Plateau by the Rudra Nubari tribe and introduced to the other peoples of Malatra by Yrtinar. Rudran spicy roast turkey recipe called for various local herbs and spices, and was served alongside roasted yams.

Magic
The obliviasphere spell cause random objects to fall from the skies, including a fully-roasted turkey.

## Rock rat

Rock rats were a mysterious species of magical rodents, possibly descended from pack rats.

Behavior
These mousy creatures were relatively shy, but also had a penchant for stealing small, shiny or brightly-colored objects of value.

Abilities
The one magical trait that was the namesake for these rodents was their uncanny ability to burrow through solid stone.

Ecology
Diet
Much like some lizards, rock rats were known for preying upon the eggs of other creatures.
Habitats
These creatures were commonly found mountains, where they riddled the stone with their burrows. Rock rats could be found in the Silver Marches, as well as around the Dragonspine Mountains in the Moonsea North and the Storm Horns mountain range in Cormyr. There they were known to be preyed upon by hedgehog shriekers.

History
According to legends, the origins of rock rats traced back to a greedy wizard who had been polymorphed into a pack rat by an angry enchantress in retaliation for swindling her. The polymorphed wizard fled into the Dragonspine Mountains, intending to live a life befitting a rodent, but all his descendants carried a bit of greed and magical nature within them.

In the, sometime around winter, the wizard Evaine and her familiar Gamaliel were traveling through the Dragonspine Mountains and chose to rest the night within a cave. Whilst Evaine was sleeping and her familiar's attention was elsewhere, a rock rat entered their cave, attracted by the glowing coals of their fire. Once its gaze met with the glittering gem of communication on her tunic, the rock rat felt compelled to carry it off into the night. Chewing through the fabric of her tunic, the rock rat freed the brooch and held it in its mouth as it scurried off through a labyrinth of tunnels that lead to a cliff face outside. Without warning, the rock rat was assailed by a snowy owl and dropped the gem of communication in its mad scramble to escape the owl's clutches. Ultimately the rodent managed to wriggle itself free of the snowy owl's grasp and fled into its burrow, letting out a sad chitter as it went. The snowy bird circled around for a moment, but shortly sensed its quarry had been lost and chose to leave the scene.

## Robe of blending

A robe of blending was a wondrous item that allowed its wearer to blend in, either by appearing as part of a wall or plant, or by appearing to be a creature of the wearer's choice.

Description
A robe of blending appeared as a completely ordinary robe and could not be detected by any form of magic. However, the moment someone put the robe on they would realize that the robe had special properties and would gain an innate understanding of how to use it.

Powers
The robe could be used to hide, either by being near-invisible or by almost perfectly mimicking any race or gender the wearer wanted. Only those of exceptional intelligence had a minuscule chance of noticing something wrong when looking at the wearer.

History
The god Hermes would on occasion create robes of blending for those he wished to reward.

Notable Owners
 Aurin, a businessman and conjurer who lived in Skullport in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.
 Karlott the Shaman, a secretive priest of a god unmentioned.

 Toraga, a samurai who wandered the lands sometime in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

 Wesclodat, a cleric of Deneir owned one of these robes.

## Roeblen

Roeblen of Bezantur, or Roeblen of Thay, was a Red Wizard of Thay in the mid-14 century DR.

Appearance
He had hair and beard cut and oiled to a razor sharp shape, and thick, powerful fingers.

Personality
The man was easily amused, making rude gestures in the mirror when by himself. He was also intensely arrogant.

Possessions
He had created a Sword of feebleminding and distributed two trapped crystal balls. He'd also managed to snatch a Staff of Silverymoon from Alustriel in ; during his fight with her, he carried an amulet that protected him against magic missile, almost certainly a brooch of shielding.

Abilities
Beyond the magic implied by the devices he'd created, he employed fireball, wall of force, disintegrate, a relatively simple spell to open doors, likely knock, and a transmuting spell that converted one of his arms into a deadly, reptilian, snaking thing about fifty feet long with claws strong enough to sever an arm, leaving wounds that quickly covered themselves with reptilian scales.

Relationships
He was part of a strange cycle-teleporting effect that allowed him to trade locations with fellow Red Wizard Thaltar Glaervar, but in return, he was bound to do the same whenever called on by Azmyrandyr, with little warning time. This cycle had been forced upon him as part of his participation in an extensive cabal; linked into the same cycle were Auvrarn Labraster and an especially large umber hulk.

History
In, Roeblen answered a call from Azmyrandyr, who employed a teleporting link to swap himself out of a fight against Alustriel Silverhand and thus escape from Silverymoon, where he'd been summoned by an umber hulk, who had in turn been called by the merchant Auvrarn Labraster, whom Alustriel believed to be hiding information regarding a murder. He did not get much time to prepare, and was barely able to snatch up his sword of feebleminding. Though he skewered her with it, it barely slowed her down.

He then hurled a fireball at the roof to create suppressive debris, and tried to open a door, but was unable to break Alustriel's extant ward on the doors with his magic. Seeing Taern Hornblade and an elf join the fray, he guarded himself with a wall of force; Alustriel's magic missile entered from above the ring-shaped force, but he took no damage thanks to an amulet. He disintegrated the wall rather than dismiss it, and then attacked with a different spell that converted one of his arms into a horrifying 50-foot snaking thing with immense claws. He managed to slice off Alustriel's arm with it, and the wound immediately became covered with snake scales as a side effect. He was able to wrest Alustriel's staff of Silverymoon from her, but thanks to a fleshfire spell she cast, his arm quickly became covered in flames; he called on the cycle to swap out for Thaltar Glaervar, tapping out of the fight.

Later, the Simbul slew him through his link to a trapped crystal ball of his' that allowed him to spy on the user. She slew also the Red Wizard Irlmarren, whom he was spying on: she shaped a spell that paralyzed them both, making smoke come out of their eyes, and then decapitated them by making the trapped balls explode. From her comments, this wasn't the first she'd heard of him.

## Rockfire

The Rockfire cataclysm was an event that destroyed a large portion of the Underdark in the late 10 century DR. It permanently closed the Underdark passages that had formerly linked Maztica and Faerûn under the Trackless Sea and forced dwarf and drow survivors to settle in Maztica.

History
Rockfire
In the legends of the desert dwarves, a large-scale war occurred between dwarves and drow across the Underdark. Somewhere in the Underdark, the drow kept a magical focus called the Darkfyre, into which they fed the bodies of their slain enemies, thus causing it to grow in power. The Darkfyre, however, ended up overwhelming those who fed it and grew by its own will into a force of widespread destruction known as the Rockfire. It burned through and destroyed vast areas of the Underdark, consuming entire cities and nations as it spread. Both sides of the conflict, the dwarves and the drow, were almost completely annihilated by the event.

Similar legends existed among the dwarves of Faerûn who remembered a great war being fought between dwarves and drow beneath the Trackless Sea, with the war ending when the drow caused an enormous fire that burned away the rocks in the seafloor and caused a breach that destroyed entire sections of the Underdark.

In the, a group of drow ambushed a dwarven mining expedition traversing tunnels west beneath the Trackless Sea from Denin's Delve in the Moonshae Isles. As dwarves and drow fought in a subterranean gallery, an eruption of a deep core fissure caused magma to spew upwards, before the ocean poured into the collapsing ceiling, killing many on both sides. Divided and thinking the other side obliterated in the catastrophe, the remaining dwarves and drow each fled westward by different routes through the Underdark, on long and perilous journeys.Ironhelm has the desert dwarf Luskag describe the Rockfire as wide-scale destruction of the Underdark, claiming whole cities and nations, whereas The Grand History of the Realms presents it as a localized volcanic eruption affecting two small groups. While it may be that the eruption was part of or caused by the Darkfyre-induced Rockfire, it seems more like that the desert dwarves' legends are misremembering or exaggerating the event: they also believe regular dwarves and drow were wiped out and the whole Underdark uninhabitable, none of which is true in Faerûn. there might be a way of partially resolving this contradiction, because Daggrande also says in Ironhelm that the Drow created a fire so enormous that it melted away the rocks and caused the sea to pour in from the ocean above. The initial explosion can be limited in size and yet still cause widespread destruction if it resulted in a breach that allowed the underdark to flood with ocean water. The implication in Niles' novels also seems to be that the same Darkfyre that caused the Rockfire was also the what caused the magma in Mount Zatal to explode on the Night of Wailing; in other words it could be both a natural event (magma explosion) and caused by the Drow mishandling the Darkfyre at the same time.

A small number of survivors of each race successfully escaped to the surface in Maztica and became surface dwellers there, permanently cut off from their brethren in Faerun and the rest of Toril.

Legacy
The event permanently destroyed the Underdark connections between Maztica and Faerûn, as well as much of the Underdark beneath Maztica. Even the caverns that were not destroyed in the fire were no longer inhabitable, because they were filled with poison gas or molten rock. It also altered the landscape of the House of Tezca above, causing new mountains to be formed and also creating the Sunstone, a mysterious lake of liquid silver that could grant visions.

The dwarves who escaped to the surface ended up living in the deserts of the House of Tezca and the Sands of Itzcala, where they became desert dwarfs, living in isolated settlements, far away from other civilizations.In another contradiction, Ironhelm has the desert dwarves in the House of Tezca and Grand History has them in the Sands of Itzcala, two widely separated deserts in the south and north respectively. They may be two separate migrating groups or have split at some point.

The drow survivors emerged in the foothills of the Axapoztlan Range and in the Valley of Nexal.In yet another apparent contradiction, Viperhelm has the drow in Nexal, whereas Grand History has them in the Axapoztlan Range, which lies next to the Sands of Itzcala, also two widely separated areas. Again, these may be two separate migrating groups, have split at some point, or moved from the latter to the former. They disguised their true identities and presented themselves as supernatural beings of power to the native humans of Maztica. They thus became the Ancient Ones who lived in the Mount Zatal volcano next to Nexal and were honored as chosen servants of the Maztican god Zaltec. They exerted great influence and power over the affairs of Nexal, and took the hearts offered at sacrificial altars to Zaltec by the Nexalans and fed them into the Darkfyre, which they preserved and kept inside Mount Zatal. This also meant that they betrayed Lolth in service to another god, but since the Rockfire had permanently separated them from Faerûn and its Underdark, the drow of Faerûn who served Lolth also had little chance to carry out her vengeance on them.

In the, a pair of runestones containing the journal of Varuna Rockjaw, one of the dwarves who experienced the Rockfire disaster, were unearthed in Itzcala by Cragwarden Samiel Tussin.

## Rosie Beestinger

Rosie Beestinger, called Grandmother Night by some, was a halfling monk and a cartographer who was a part of Acquisitions Incorporated "C" Team in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Rosie was a grandmotherly yet feisty and sassy woman of studious and schooled demeanor. She had a light skin wrinkled with advanced age and long gray hair she usually kept braided. In 1496 DR, Rosie was 110 winters old. She was a petite woman of only  but she was far from defenseless.

Abilities
As an experienced monk, Rosie could disappear into the shadows only to pummel an unaware enemy. She was proficient at deflecting incoming missiles, and her training granted her the benefits of having a timeless body. Rosie also possessed certain innate spellcasting abilities that allowed her to cast such spells as minor illusion, sacred flame, thaumaturgy, command, darkness, darkvision, pass without trace, and silence.

Possessions

Rosie carried a set of cartographer's tools branded with Acquisitions Incorporated logo. Around her neck, the hin wore an enchanted necklace of the greater will composed of massive beads. A magic belt of the Beestinger was wrapped around Rosie's waist, guiding her blows towards their target. She wielded the Beestinger staff, a gift from Walnut Dankgrass, and on other occasions a staff of the master. Other items found on her person were Rosie's collection of eyeballs stuffed in a jar and a bagful of emeralds.

Relationships
Rosie Beestinger was a member of an uncannily numerous halfling family. The number of children she'd had throughout her life, both born and adopted, was staggering. Pendragon Beestinger was one of Rosie's adopted children who embraced the family's alleged criminal activities and once attempted to assassinate his mother only to be killed in turn. Later, Pendragon Beestinger was resurrected at Rosie's request.

History
Rosie, along with Walnut Dankgrass, Donaar Blit'zen, and K'thriss Drow'b, was hand-picked by Omin Dran, the founder of Acquisitions Incorporated, to form the organization's "C" Team.

In the, Rosie Beestinger resided in the Dran & Courtier inn in the town of Red Larch. Her room was quite spartan: nothing more than a mat, map sheaths, and a training dummy. Along with the "C" Team, Rosie became nothing short of a legendary individual amongst the hin.

Rumors & Legends
Rosie and her family had a reputation beyond that of a heroic adventuress. There were rumors that alleged Rosie's connection to a number of nefarious halfling criminal organizations, however, none of the rumors was proven to be true and Rosie herself claimed to possess no knowledge of any shady dealings nor criminal activities, despite her nickname "Grandmother Night."

Around 1374 DR, Rosella and her mother Valanice joined the Myth Drannor outskirts camp to examine the relics brought out of the ruins. One day, Rosella wandered off on her own to find adventure, and instead of death found a perfectly intact shortbow that defied the typical state of things. ## Romantic Dreams Forays

Romantic Dreams Forays was an entertainment business based in the small village of Halfcrag in the 14 century DR, operated entirely by a band of semi-retired female adventurers, that gave patrons the experience of being an adventurer through a largely pre-planned theatrical excursion.

Activities
The Foray charged a stiff fee for its excursions, an advanced fee of 50 gold pieces; they were once given a rating of  by Volothamp Geddarm on his rating system. Their resultant clientele were often wealthy Sembians seeking some excitement. They even received repeat customers, who enjoyed the outings regardless of whether or not they figured out how staged everything was.

The Foray experience began in a steep-sided dell, out of sight of Halfcrag, wherein patrons would be fitted for equipment and put through some relaxed weapon training. As evening rolled around a campfire would be started and a number of alcoholic beverages would be offered to patrons. Around the fire Foray guides would tell their patrons scary stories of dangers that lurked around the nearby Dun Hills.

At dawn the clientele and their Foray guides would ride out on horseback, either on the most scenic trails or into the very heart of the highest hill, and encounter an ample supply of monster tracks that had been laid yesterday by other members of the company. The group would then be attacked by two creatures that had likewise been pre-arranged by the Foray guides, a devenomed serpent and a stirge whose wings were clipped, to give the customers "monster trophies" to take home. Afterwards the guides would bring the band of "adventurers" to a hidden pond to rest and unwind with a swim, as well as a relaxed highsun meal. And for those clients whom the guides found to be attractive enough, they would some amorous advances – according to Volothamp, Foray guide Anjanna told him this was what constituted the "dreams" part of their company's name.

Once well rested, the band's monster trophies would be secured to their saddles and make their way to exploring a nearby ruin, the crumbling remains of an ancient keep. Upon a staircase within this keep laid bones, allegedly belonging to fallen warriors, and a handful of coins that the Foray guides had scattered amongst them a day prior. At that point a prepared illusion spell would be triggered, projecting a menacing ghostly visage. The guides would refer to it as being a "mythical deathghost" and warn their patrons to stay back, saying they "have not the magic to defeat it", and demonstrate how it goes unfazed by turn undead.

As the Foray band inevitably made a retreat from the ruined keep, an illusion of a snarling wyvern would swoop down at them. The retreat would bring them rushing into a valley, where an illusion of sixteen masked bandits would ambush them, charging forth with shouts of a challenge. The Foray band would not engage with the outlaws, whose illusion was set up to never actually catch the fleeing "adventurers". At last the company would make a triumphant return to their campground at the base of Halfcrag, just in time to enjoy evenfeast.

Tactics
Whenever the Foray guides recognized one or more of those signed up for an excursion were prior patrons, they would try to vary up their scripted encounters. The women were also more then prepared to handle rogues or actual monsters wandering into their set route, as happened on ocassion.

Members
The membership of the Romantic Dreams Forays was made up rather attractive human and half-elven women who had partially retired from life as adventurers. Whenever they went out on one of their tours members carried a robe of useful items, as well as a bountiful supply of elixirs of health and potions of healing.

History
When Volothamp Geddarm visited with the members of Romantic Dreams Forays in his travels across the Dalelands, they recounted one incident in which a pair of firedrakes pounced upon them. A guide was badly injured and the jaded Sembian patrons in their company uproariously lunged at the dragonets, slaughtering them with reckless abandon.

## Roonsundyr's Warren

Roonsundyr's Warren was a private residence in the Central Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The entrance to the warren consisted of an ornately sculpted balcony situated within the cavern, fronted by a permanent prismatic wall that barred the archway beyond.

Defenses
The chambers beyond the entrance were reputed to be heavily fortified with an array of mechanical and magical traps of exceptional complexity, said to rival the skills of even a grandmaster thief. The internal layout and exact nature of the residence remained undocumented, as no known individual had successfully entered the warren and returned.

Inhabitants
Roonsundyr's Warren was the private residence of one of Skullport's most reclusive figures, the eccentric archmage Othur Roonsundyr. Roonsundyr did not receive visitors under any circumstances. He typically emerged from his sanctum no more than once or twice per month. On such occasions, he frequented the Trade Lanes, conducting transactions with gemstones as currency. Upon completion of his business, he returned to his residence, transporting his purchases through the air by magical means.

## Roost of the Moonclaws

The Roost of the Moonclaws was the base of the Moonclaws gang of Hastarl in the early 3 century DR.

Location
The Roost of the Moonclaws was in the zone of the city with upscale houses, but which were not yet so wealthy as to have their own baths.

Structure
The place had a main hall with a number of tubs.

Services
Customers could get baths at tubs, with scented soaps.

Activities
Junior members of the Moonclaws gang scrubbed bathtubs there, in addition to their usual gang activities.

History
In, shortly after the elimination of the Velvet Hands rival thieves' gang, the Moonclaws were attacked by the last survivor of the Velvet Hands, Elminster Aumar, who slew a great number of theirs in an ambush at a warehouse. They set the warehouse on fire with him inside, and he responded by escaping and torching the home of Isparla, leader of the Moonclaws. She retreated to the Roost, where he followed her forces.

At the Roost, he ambushed the thieves, killing one. Before he managed another kill, the Magister of Mystra, Dorgon Heamiiolothtar, intervened and froze time for most of them, biding him flee and attack no other foe if he'd see Farl, the leader of the Velvet Hands, and Tassabra, another member, alive. Elminster took his offer and left while they were frozen.

## Roast thuldur bites

Roast thuldur bites was a drow meat dish commonly served in the first course of a drow banquet.

Description
The meat of this dish was derived from old pack lizards. The throats of large, old specimens would traditionally be sliced and then left to bled out, as pack lizard blood could be used for a variety of culinary purposes. The body would then be sliced into long ribbons and overcooked in a boiling broth of various mushrooms in order to soften the meat. Finally, the meat would be cut into small, bite-sized pieces and run onto backbone skewers.

The great bear was said to be so large that it could devour whole families in a single bite. The tales generally agreed that it was an unkillable ghostly apparition, and some also claimed that it could assume the shape of a human man.

Activities
The Roaming Bear was said to pace across the highlands of the Moonshaes on misty nights, looking for victims to eat.

The legend of the Roaming Bear originated in the realm of Gnarhelm before spreading to other Northlander lands.

## Rolf Olafsson

King Rolf Olafsson was ruler of the Northlander kingdom of Olafstaad during the early-to-mid 14 century DR, and subsequently the king of all of Gnarhelm in the Moonshae Isles.

Personality
He was more cosmopolitan than the average Northlander king, having an eye for art as well as a respect for book learning. He maintained a collection of art and valuables (acquired mostly through raiding) that he curated tastefully.

Relationships
His son and heir was Svenyird Olafsson, whom he sent to study under Elminster when the boy was young.

History
Circa, Rolf received word that the city of Gnarhelm had been left undefended by King Sigurd Helmudson and was under attack by pirates. He led his cavalry on an overnight ride to the city, fearing that he would be too late to do anything but avenge the town. Instead, he found that the city had been saved, and there met Elminster during the sage's tour of the Moonshae Isles. Rolf invited Elminster back to his home at Olafstaad, where he entertained the sage for nearly a tenday before arranging to take him to Waterdeep via longship.

When King Helmudson died in the, Rolf Olafsson assumed his throne. He ruled over Gnarhelm, Olafstaad, and Sunderstaad.

In the, King Olafsson was witness to the signing of the Treaty of Oman, ending the centuries-long open hostilities between the Northlanders and the Ffolk.

He died of natural causes the following year, and was succeeded by Svenyird.

## Romanisk Vlaensk

Romanisk Vlaensk was a vampire that took over Stag Falls in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Abilities
Romanisk Vlaensk had no shortage of spells, certainly including alacrity, burning hands, color spray, cone of cold, disguise undead, dispel magic, dispel silence, feeblemind, flame arrow, flaming sphere, glitterdust, grease, guards and wards, hold portal, Imbue undead with spell ability, invisibility, ironguard, magic missile, minor globe of invulnerability, minor spell turning, mirror image, revenance, stoneskin, watchwere and web.

He may have also possessed other spells such as Bloodstone's frightful joining, charm monster, globe of invulnerability, hold monster, ice storm, ironguard, Mordenkainen's magnificent mansion, screen, spell engine, spell turning, lightning bolt, and even Mordenkainen's disjunction.

Possessions
Notably, the vampire had a crystal parrot. His spellbook could not be found.

Activities
After being trapped underground, but before taking over Stag Falls, Romanisk studied magic in his underground abode for centuries.

Relationships
The vampire had turned Vilhelm von Stag Falls into an undead in his thrall, and also charmed Doreta, a local young woman, for companionship. He was also served by an umber hulk, and a killer mimic. He was not known by name to anyone else, and kept numerous undead in thrall.

History
Centuries before the 14 century DR, Romanisk Vlaensk was a vampire and the master of the castle of the future Stag Falls village; his heraldic device was ermines with a red circle. However, he was defeated, and forced to retreat into the foundations of his castle, which his foes collapsed, sharing the tale so that no one would try and dig in there. He spent centuries underground, merely experimenting with magic, until released by the excavations commissioned by Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls, who was investigating the history of the region. The baron's line had rebuilt Romanisk's castle and refounded the surrounding village; Vilhelm had the Baron, whom he turned into an undead creature, charm his staff and bring Romanisk live villagers to feed on. Until his uncovering, the mage Calmishandra resided in the castle.

Among other acts, Romanisk summoned bats, stranding the werebat Minsk from the Demiplane of Dread in the Prime Material Plane, had the local church of Torm destroyed, the local blacksmith, Churles, brought to the castle to outfit the army he meant to raise, the local huntmaster, Jeagermeister, killed, along with his dogs, for baying too loudly in his presence, had two other villagers brought to him, driving one of them to suicide, charmed a local young woman, Doreta, for company, had a local man, Darrold, taken hostage to ensure bowyer Clarrisa's services in outfitting his army, and then killed him anyway when he expressed horror at the Baron's undeath. Romanks drank his blood and animated him as a zombie, using a spell to hide this from casual observation. He had animated many others he made meals of.

When adventurers from Ravens Bluff were finally sent to investigate why the village had missed its taxes, about one month into Romanisk's reign, they eventually defeated the undead Vilhelm von Stag Falls, and proceeded into the catacombs to challenge Romanisk. He had fortified the caverns in preparation.

## Roquern

Roquern was the secret ruler of the island of Jacaman in the Korinn Archipelago of the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
He was a devil who used magic to disguise his true nature, choosing to appear as a Ffolk gentleman.

Abilities
Roquern used his infernal powers to enslave mortals, either by offering them infernal deals in which they traded away their freedom (and/or their souls) or by having them consume spices which he had tainted with his power. Consuming even a small amount of these tainted spices rendered a mortal more susceptible to coercion to perform evil acts, while consuming a large amount turned them into a thrall under Roquern's control.

Activities
By the late 15 century DR, Roquern controlled the growing, harvesting, processing, and exporting of a variety of valuable spices which were unique to the island of Jacaman and which he tainted with his infernal powers. His spice plantations were operated by a combination of his slaves as well as evil creatures whom he had recruited.

History
Roquern seized control over Jacaman sometime in the 15 century DR. By the, his schemes were fully operational and he was taking great pains to convince the outside world that Jacaman was just another insignificant island as he exported his tainted spices. By this time, none had yet discovered the truth about Jacaman spice.

## Rogue of Waterdeep

Waterdavian rogues were rogues from Waterdeep, renowned for their skill in evading the City Watch of the City of Splendors.

Culture
A trait universally ascribed to Waterdhavian rogues was a mild paranoia. This is understandable given their history of persecution by the Lords of Waterdeep. Other than that, each rogue could differ from one another as much as any two members of the same race differed from one another.

Because they lived in a city where the great tide of humanoid races washed through and left its mark on every citizen, Waterdhavian rogues often found themselves with both many friends and potentially some life-long rivals. Younger rogues were often taken into the apprenticeship of older and more experienced ones.

Abilities
These rogues had a tendency to specialize in a single skill to a greater extent than most other rogues. They were also proficient in a larger amount of skills than most other rogues.

Notable Waterdavian Rogues
 Madame Beautiful, a comely but egotistical rogue.
 Stalker, an ebon-clad rogue.

Appendix
References

## Rogue of Evermeet

Rogues of Evermeet were elven rogues from Evermeet that practiced roguish arts specialized to a forest setting.

Activities
These rogues engaged in patrolling the forests.

Culture
Evermeet's rogues were introspective and thoughtful. They were comfortable living in the wilderness and in handling animals. There were among the most rustic people in Faerûn, apart from druids and rangers.

Possessions
They wore buckskin clothing and eagle-feather adornments. They typically donned cloaks of elvenkind, which let them without through the forests without being seen.

Abilities
These rogues were proficient in animal handling, animal lore, hunting, and tracking.

They were very effective at sneaking in forest areas as well as detecting sounds in them.

However, their lack of contact with large urban areas made them less effective in them. They were more prone to making mistakes in such places.

## Roffas Calindor

Roffas Calindor was a man of Tethyr and a member of the Knights of the Shield in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Relationships
He was married to Melek, who was the younger sister of Nurih al Suhal, a moneylender of Teshburl and member of the Rundeen in Calimshan by 1370 DR.

## Road to Tarath Marad

The road to Tarath Marad was a series of underground passages within Eberron's underworld of Khyber, which led to Tarath Marad and the Spinner's Prison.

Description

The road was a twisting and circuitous path through many caverns and tunnels, starting from near the Harbor of Stormreach in Xen'drik and then passing to the depths of Khyber. The passageways were lined with many dragonshards that glowed in the darkness, and the deeper reaches were lined with enormous and thick spider webs. Many areas were partially or wholly blocked off by flows of molten magma, chasms, and cave-ins. Further obstacles were posed by active magical barriers created by the servants of the Silver Flame, which could only be broken with great difficulty by the forces of evil.

The tunnels were inhabited by ferocious and deadly fiends, including hell hounds, hezrou, and fire reavers. The fire reavers were especially fond of the magma pools.

History
The Spinner's Prison was created during the Age of Demons, when the Silver Flame trapped the Spinner of Shadows in the depths of the earth and bound her with the Silver Flame Guardian. Outside the prison on the road, a servant of the Silver Flame was teleported in from the surface and maintained the various magical wards that kept the prison together and prevented outside interference. Brother Aurelius was the latest attendant, and lived in a homey nook near the prison.

Around the time of the Darkening and the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, the Lords of Dust in Stormreach performed a great ritual that blasted open a hole in the surface of the Harbor and opened up the formerly hidden road to Tarath Marad, seeking to find and free the Spinner of Shadows below. The Church of the Silver Flame, the Twelve, and the Gatekeepers all arrived to stop the Lords of Dust, but were slowed by the many fanatical cultists and the demons they brought to bear. While the cultists harvested the local dragonshards for the Spinner, they were also predated upon by the local fiends, such as the Fire Reaver Lord, who found them quite tasty.

## Rondo Marsh

Rondo Marsh was a ship mage in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, who was briefly involved in a mission to create an alliance between Selgaunt and Myth Nantar.

Description
Rondo Marsh was tanned and freckled, red-haired, green-eyed,  and .

Personality
Rondo was a relentlessly hard and loyal worker who bore an intense hatred for the rich and powerful. He was not completely comfortable with the position of ship's mage, because of the implications of wealth and education that it had.

Abilities
In spite of his competence as a wizard, Rondo Marsh was not actually very bright. His hardiness, however, was beyond heroic, and when avenging friends, he had the strength (and recklessness) of a berserker. He also had developed many important seamanship skills, such as fishing, swimming, weather predictions, underwater spellcasting, and even spear wielding.

Most notably, his spellbook contained gust of wind, lightning bolt, and water breathing.

Possessions
Rondo Marsh's spellbook was one of Boccob's blessed books, and he carried a potion of water breathing. His only other possessions of note were an ointment that protected him against sunburns and an oiled canvas poncho.

Activities
He was the ship mage of the Emerald Tayar.

Relationships
Rondo Marsh worked for Coprates Bile, a pirate captain. He was infatuated with Ilsa Merrymore, to the point he was capable of flying into a berserker rage if she was hurt.

During the expedition, he took an intense dislike to Sturge Whitsunday, an important paladin and politician. The paladin's sister Aleine proved an exception to his scorn for upper-class people, on the other hand. The inventor Nagreous Twiddle, he just thought of as a very funny little man with big words.

History
Born in abject poverty in Selgaunt, Rondo Marsh eventually became an oarsman on a Sembian trader. He had made it an habit of working himself like a mule by his mid-teens, a trait only matched by his dislike for the wealthy and influential.

At the age of eighteen, he stole a wizard's spellbook, and realized he could cast some spells, which got him abandoned on shore by his old crew. He signed on to the Emerald Tayar under Captain Coprates Bile. He worked hard to learn more spells as the ship's mage.

During an expedition in Mirtul of the, in which the Emerald Tayar's crew were hired to find a hidden underwater city by the description of Myth Nantar, and bring Sturge Whitsunday there so that a mutual defense agreement could be reached, Rondo Marsh was part of the crew.
